## Numbers 1:1 (id: 22602)

* In the year after Israel left Egypt, the Lord often communicated his will to the people through Moses, giving them many chances to follow his divine plan.
* God often revealed himself *in* *the Tent of Meeting*; see study note on Exodus 27:21.
* The wilderness of Sinai was a vast, frightening, and harsh desert ([Deuteronomy 1:19](https://ref.ly/Deut1:19)). This mostly empty area was where God matured Israel. The challenges of Sinai's harsh living conditions tested Israel's reliance on the Lord throughout the book of Numbers. Sinai reminded later biblical authors of the law and Israel's rebellious spirit ([Psalm 106:19](https://ref.ly/Ps106:19); [Hebrews 12:25–26](https://ref.ly/Heb12:25-Heb12:26)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 1:1
* **Associated Articles:** Exodus 27:21 (ID: `11234@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Wilderness of Sinai (ID: `place:SinaiWilderness`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`)

## Numbers 1:2–16 (id: 22603)

* *those who are twenty years of age or older by their divisions—everyone who can serve in Israel’s army:* This registration gathered more information than the earlier census (compare [Exodus 38:26](https://ref.ly/Exod38:26)).
* *These are the names of the men who are to assist you:* Hebrew culture was tribal, centered around clans and families. They easily identified the tribal leaders, who appear again as a group in [Numbers 7](https://ref.ly/Num7:1-Num7:89).
* Moses and Aaron did not include the tribe of Levi in this registration because it had a special status in Israelite society (see [Numbers 1:47–53](https://ref.ly/Num1:47-Num1:53); [26:51–62](https://ref.ly/Num26:51-Num26:62); [Deuteronomy 18:5](https://ref.ly/Deut18:5); [33:8–11](https://ref.ly/Deut33:8-Deut33:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 1:2-16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Ammishaddai (ID: `person:Ammishaddai`); Abidan (ID: `person:Abidan`); Ahiezer (ID: `person:Ahiezer`); Shelumiel (ID: `person:Shelumiel`); Nethanel (ID: `person:Nethanel`); Shedeur (ID: `person:Shedeur`); Pedahzur (ID: `person:Pedahzur`); Amminadab (ID: `person:Amminadab`); Ochran (ID: `person:Ochran`); Ammihud (ID: `person:Ammihud`); Elishama (ID: `person:Elishama`); Elizur (ID: `person:Elizur`); Eliasaph (ID: `person:Eliasaph`); Zurishaddai (ID: `person:Zurishaddai`); Gideoni (ID: `person:Gideoni`); Deuel (ID: `person:Deuel`); Helon (ID: `person:Helon`); Enan (ID: `person:Enan`); Gamaliel (ID: `person:Gamaliel.2`); Pagiel (ID: `person:Pagiel`); Eliab (ID: `person:Eliab`); Zuar (ID: `person:Zuar`); Ahira (ID: `person:Ahira`); Reuben (ID: `person:Reuben`); Naphtali (ID: `person:Naphtali`); Asher (ID: `person:Asher`); Ephraim (ID: `person:Ephraim`); Tribe of Asher (ID: `group:Asher`); Tribe of Issachar (ID: `group:Issachar`); Tribe of Dan (ID: `group:Dan`); Tribe of Judah (ID: `group:Judah`); Simeon (ID: `person:Simeon.5`); Tribe of Simeon (ID: `group:Simeon`); Dan (ID: `place:Dan`); Gad (ID: `person:Gad`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Benjamin (ID: `person:Benjamin`); Tribe of Naphtali (ID: `group:Naphtali`); Tribe of Reuben (ID: `group:Reuben`); Tribe of Benjamin (ID: `group:Benjamin`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Tribe of Zebulun (ID: `group:Zebulun`); Tribe of Ephraim (ID: `group:Ephraim`); Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`); Manasseh (ID: `person:Manasseh`); Issachar (ID: `person:Issachar`); Zebulun (ID: `person:Zebulun`); Tribe of Manasseh (ID: `group:Manasseh`); Nahshon (ID: `person:Nahshon`)

## Numbers 1:17–46 (id: 22601)

The count of 603,550 shows the same number of qualified men as mentioned in [Exodus 38:26](https://ref.ly/Exod38:26). [Exodus 12:37](https://ref.ly/Exod12:37) and [Numbers 11:21](https://ref.ly/Num11:21) give an approximate number of 600,000\. 

People have interpreted these numbers in different ways:

* The numbers might show the actual size of this army (see [Exodus 1:9–10](https://ref.ly/Exod1:9-Exod1:10)).
* The numbers might be symbolic. A later editor might have estimated, or might have misunderstood while translating.

See also study note on Exodus 12:37.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 1:17-46
* **Associated Articles:** Exodus 12:37 (ID: `11027@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Reuben (ID: `person:Reuben`); Naphtali (ID: `person:Naphtali`); Asher (ID: `person:Asher`); Ephraim (ID: `person:Ephraim`); Tribe of Asher (ID: `group:Asher`); Tribe of Dan (ID: `group:Dan`); Simeon (ID: `person:Simeon.5`); Tribe of Judah (ID: `group:Judah`); Tribe of Simeon (ID: `group:Simeon`); Dan (ID: `place:Dan`); Gad (ID: `person:Gad`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Benjamin (ID: `person:Benjamin`); Tribe of Naphtali (ID: `group:Naphtali`); Tribe of Reuben (ID: `group:Reuben`); Tribe of Benjamin (ID: `group:Benjamin`); Tribe of Zebulun (ID: `group:Zebulun`); Tribe of Ephraim (ID: `group:Ephraim`); Manasseh (ID: `person:Manasseh`); Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`); Issachar (ID: `person:Issachar`); Zebulun (ID: `person:Zebulun`); Tribe of Manasseh (ID: `group:Manasseh`); Tribe of Issachar (ID: `group:Issachar`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Wilderness of Sinai (ID: `place:SinaiWilderness`)

## Numbers 1:47–54 (id: 22605)

The tribe of Levi did not serve in Israel's army like the other tribes. This part of Numbers explains how the Levites organized to serve in the tabernacle of the Testimony, which was their main duty.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 1:47-54

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Levi (ID: `person:Levi.3`); Tribe of Levi (ID: `group:Levi`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Testimony (ID: `keyterm:Witness.2`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Appoint (ID: `keyterm:Appoint`); Standard (ID: `realia:Standard`)

## Numbers 1:50 (id: 22607)

*tabernacle of the Testimony:* This was a portable sanctuary. It symbolized the Lord’s presence and showed Israel’s covenant relationship with him.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 1:50

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Appoint (ID: `keyterm:Appoint`); Levi (ID: `person:Levi.3`); Testimony (ID: `keyterm:Witness.2`); Tribe of Levi (ID: `group:Levi`)

## Numbers 1:50–53 (id: 22606)

The Levites had the job of setting up, taking down, moving, and guarding the tabernacle. They kept unauthorized people from coming too close or making it unclean. This protected the Israelites from the Lord's judgment.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 1:50-53

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Levi (ID: `person:Levi.3`); Tribe of Levi (ID: `group:Levi`); Testimony (ID: `keyterm:Witness.2`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Appoint (ID: `keyterm:Appoint`); Standard (ID: `realia:Standard`)

## Numbers 2:1–34 (id: 22740)

Israel organized its camps by tribal groups. This probably addressed social status, water access, and security for the Tent of Meeting. This tent represented the Lord's presence. The Egyptian army from around the same time period used a similar defensive formation to protect holy objects during their battle campaigns.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 2:1-34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Standard (ID: `realia:Standard`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Reuben (ID: `person:Reuben`); Levi (ID: `person:Levi.3`); Ephraim (ID: `person:Ephraim`); Tribe of Dan (ID: `group:Dan`); Tribe of Judah (ID: `group:Judah`); Dan (ID: `place:Dan`); Tribe of Reuben (ID: `group:Reuben`); Tribe of Ephraim (ID: `group:Ephraim`); Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`); Tribe of Levi (ID: `group:Levi`); Abidan (ID: `person:Abidan`); Ahiezer (ID: `person:Ahiezer`); Shelumiel (ID: `person:Shelumiel`); Shedeur (ID: `person:Shedeur`); Nethanel (ID: `person:Nethanel`); Ochran (ID: `person:Ochran`); Ammihud (ID: `person:Ammihud`); Elishama (ID: `person:Elishama`); Elizur (ID: `person:Elizur`); Eliasaph (ID: `person:Eliasaph`); Zurishaddai (ID: `person:Zurishaddai`); Gideoni (ID: `person:Gideoni`); Deuel (ID: `person:Deuel`); Helon (ID: `person:Helon`); Enan (ID: `person:Enan`); Eliab (ID: `person:Eliab`); Zuar (ID: `person:Zuar`); Ahira (ID: `person:Ahira`); Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`); Naphtali (ID: `person:Naphtali`); Asher (ID: `person:Asher`); Tribe of Asher (ID: `group:Asher`); Tribe of Issachar (ID: `group:Issachar`); Ammishaddai (ID: `person:Ammishaddai`); Pedahzur (ID: `person:Pedahzur`); Amminadab (ID: `person:Amminadab`); Simeon (ID: `person:Simeon.5`); Tribe of Simeon (ID: `group:Simeon`); Gad (ID: `person:Gad`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Benjamin (ID: `person:Benjamin`); Tribe of Naphtali (ID: `group:Naphtali`); Tribe of Benjamin (ID: `group:Benjamin`); Tribe of Zebulun (ID: `group:Zebulun`); Gamaliel (ID: `person:Gamaliel.2`); Manasseh (ID: `person:Manasseh`); Issachar (ID: `person:Issachar`); Pagiel (ID: `person:Pagiel`); Zebulun (ID: `person:Zebulun`); Tribe of Manasseh (ID: `group:Manasseh`); Nahshon (ID: `person:Nahshon`)

## Numbers 2:2 (id: 22744)

* Each area of the Hebrew camp had family banners, probably flags or streamers ([1:52](https://ref.ly/Num1:52)). Modern Bedouins also use tribal symbols or battle flags. This shows the continued importance of tribe, clan, and family divisions.
* The tribes camped around the holy place at a safe distance (compare [Joshua 3:4](https://ref.ly/Josh3:4)). This respected the distinction between God's holiness and the non\-Levitical tribes.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 2:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Standard (ID: `realia:Standard`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`)

## Numbers 2:3–9 (id: 22745)

The tribes of Judah, Issachar, and Zebulun camped in the most important spot on the east side of the Tent of Meeting. They led when Israel traveled. This largest tribal group had 186,400 people. It could protect the holy tent and its assistants very well.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 2:3-9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`); Tribe of Judah (ID: `group:Judah`); Eliab (ID: `person:Eliab`); Nethanel (ID: `person:Nethanel`); Zuar (ID: `person:Zuar`); Helon (ID: `person:Helon`); Tribe of Zebulun (ID: `group:Zebulun`); Tribe of Issachar (ID: `group:Issachar`); Issachar (ID: `person:Issachar`); Amminadab (ID: `person:Amminadab`); Zebulun (ID: `person:Zebulun`); Nahshon (ID: `person:Nahshon`); Standard (ID: `realia:Standard`)

## Numbers 3:1–4 (id: 22872)

Aaron's family line provided the priests who led Israel's worship. Aaron's four sons were the core of Israel's priesthood. They served in God's presence on behalf of the people. God chose (ordained) them to lead and serve as ministers in the Tent of Meeting. They also acted as guardians of God's covenant with his people.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 3:1-4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Nadab (ID: `person:Nadab`); Ithamar (ID: `person:Ithamar`); Abihu (ID: `person:Abihu`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Eleazar (ID: `person:Eleazar.2`); Priesthood (ID: `keyterm:Priesthood`); Smear (ID: `keyterm:Smear`); Wilderness of Sinai (ID: `place:SinaiWilderness`); Mount Sinai (ID: `place:SinaiMount`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`)

## Numbers 3:1–4.49 (id: 22871)

Israel's priests and Levites had duties like moving, assembling, protecting, and maintaining the Tent of Meeting. The way God organized the tribes highlighted his concern for these priestly tasks.

## Numbers 3:4 (id: 22882)

* The deaths of *Nadab and Abihu* (see [Leviticus 10:1–2](https://ref.ly/Lev10:1-Lev10:2)) showed the need to protect the holiness of the sanctuary and to strictly obey God's instructions ([Numbers 26:61](https://ref.ly/Num26:61); [Leviticus 16:1](https://ref.ly/Lev16:1); [1 Chronicles 24:2](https://ref.ly/1Chr24:2); compare [Acts 5:1–11](https://ref.ly/Acts5:1-Acts5:11); [1 Corinthians 10:6–11](https://ref.ly/1Cor10:6-1Cor10:11); [11:29–30](https://ref.ly/1Cor11:29-1Cor11:30)).
* *Eleazar and Ithamar:* See “Eleazar” Profile.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 3:4
* **Associated Articles:** Eleazar (ID: `500472@TyndaleStudyNotesProfiles`)

## Numbers 3:5–13 (id: 22883)

God chose the Levites to be assistants for the priests, represented by Aaron. They had a number of holy duties in their ministry service. They performed holy tasks to maintain the Tent of Meeting and its items ([3:7](https://ref.ly/Num3:7)[,8](https://ref.ly/Num3:8); explained more in [4:1–33](https://ref.ly/Num4:1-Num4:33)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 3:5-13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Levi (ID: `person:Levi.3`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Tribe of Levi (ID: `group:Levi`); Force to Serve (ID: `keyterm:EnticedToServe`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Priesthood (ID: `keyterm:Priesthood`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`)

## Numbers 3:10 (id: 22873)

The sanctuary was set apart for God’s holy use ([Numbers 1:51](https://ref.ly/Num1:51); [3:38](https://ref.ly/Num3:38); [18:7](https://ref.ly/Num18:7)). Only qualified people could approach it (compare [3:4](https://ref.ly/Num3:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 3:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Priesthood (ID: `keyterm:Priesthood`)

## Numbers 3:11–13 (id: 22874)

* *every firstborn Israelite*: These belonged to God because God spared them when the Egyptian firstborn sons died ([3:13](https://ref.ly/Num3:13); see [Exodus 11–12](https://ref.ly/Exod11:1-Exod12:51)).
* *I* *have taken the Levites:* God chose the Levites to serve as substitutes for the firstborn sons.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 3:11-13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Levi (ID: `person:Levi.3`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Tribe of Levi (ID: `group:Levi`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`)

## Numbers 3:14–39 (id: 22875)

* This first phase of a two\-part registration of the tribe of Levi summarized each family's special duties. [4:1–33](https://ref.ly/Num4:1-Num4:33) describes this in more detail (see [4:34–49](https://ref.ly/Num4:34-Num4:49) for the second phase). This count did not relate to military duties, so it included male Levites from the age of one month ([3:40–41](https://ref.ly/Num3:40-Num3:41)).
* Three family groups descend from the three sons of Levi: Gershon, Kohath, and Merari. These clans, often called the Gershonites, Kohathites, and Merarites, later inherited the "cities of refuge" ([Joshua 21](https://ref.ly/Josh21:1-Josh21:45)). They also had various important roles in the Jerusalem temple ([2 Chronicles 29:3–17](https://ref.ly/2Chr29:3-2Chr29:17)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 3:14-39

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Gershon (ID: `person:Gershon`); Merari (ID: `person:Merari`); Kohath (ID: `person:Kohath`); Levi (ID: `person:Levi.3`); Tribe of Levi (ID: `group:Levi`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Gershonites (ID: `group:Gershon`); Uzziel (ID: `person:Uzziel`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Screen (ID: `realia:Screen`); Hebron (ID: `person:Hebron`); Amram (ID: `person:Amram`); Izhar (ID: `person:Izhar`); Shimei (ID: `person:Shimei.4`); Mushi (ID: `person:Mushi`); Mahli (ID: `person:Mahli`); Libni (ID: `person:Libni`); Kohathites (ID: `group:Kohath`); Elizaphan (ID: `person:Elizaphan`); Shimeites (ID: `group:Shimei`); Zuriel (ID: `person:Zuriel`); Abihail (ID: `person:Abihail`); Amramites (ID: `group:Amram`); Izharites (ID: `group:Izhar`); Mahlites (ID: `group:Mahli`); Uzzielites (ID: `group:Uzziel`); Libnites (ID: `group:Libni`); Mushites (ID: `group:Mushi`); Lael (ID: `person:Lael`); Hebronites (ID: `group:Hebron`); Eliasaph (ID: `person:Eliasaph.2`); Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`); Temple Altar (ID: `realia:TempleAltar`); Rope (ID: `realia:Rope`); Stone Altar (ID: `realia:StoneAltar`); Tabernacle Altar (ID: `realia:TabernacleAltar`); Altar (ID: `realia:Altar`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Eleazar (ID: `person:Eleazar.2`); Covenant Box (ID: `keyterm:CovenantBox`); Wilderness of Sinai (ID: `place:SinaiWilderness`); Perimiter Curtain (ID: `realia:PerimiterCurtain`); Ark of the Covenant (ID: `realia:ArkOfTheCovenant`); Peg (ID: `realia:Peg.2`); Peg (ID: `realia:Peg`); Frame (ID: `realia:Frame`); Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`); Lampstand (ID: `realia:Lampstand.2`); Covering (ID: `realia:Covering`)

## Numbers 3:27–32 (id: 22876)

Aaron and Moses, sons of Amram, were descendants of Kohath (see [Exodus 6:18](https://ref.ly/Exod6:18), [20](https://ref.ly/Exod6:20), [26](https://ref.ly/Exod6:26)). They were caretakers of the Most Holy Place and its very holy furnishings (described in [Numbers 3–4](https://ref.ly/Num3:1-Num4:49)). The Kohathites were the most respected of the three clans.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 3:27-32

## Numbers 3:32 (id: 22878)

Aaron’s third son, Eleazar, led the Levites as an administrator. He was like an executive secretary, while his father did other tasks. He had "oversight of the entire tabernacle" ([4:16–20](https://ref.ly/Num4:16-Num4:20)). Eleazar had a key role during Israel’s time in the wilderness. He later became the high priest after his father (see [16:37–39](https://ref.ly/Num16:37-Num16:39); [19:3–6](https://ref.ly/Num19:3-Num19:6); [20:25–28](https://ref.ly/Num20:25-Num20:28); [26:1–3](https://ref.ly/Num26:1-Num26:3)).

## Numbers 3:40–51 (id: 22881)

There were 273 more firstborn sons in Israel from the non\-Levite tribes than there were Levite males (compare [Leviticus 3:39](https://ref.ly/Num3:39)). The Levites acted as substitutes for Israel's firstborn sons ([3:41](https://ref.ly/Num3:41); see study note on 3:11–13). The law required a redemption price of five pieces of silver for each extra firstborn son ([Numbers 3:46–48](https://ref.ly/Num3:46-Num3:48); compare [Leviticus 27:6](https://ref.ly/Lev27:6)). 

It is unclear who paid this cost. This arrangement for special substitutes points to Christ’s role as a substitute. Through his death, Christ paid the redemption price for all humanity (see [Mark 10:45](https://ref.ly/Mark10:45); [Galatians 3:13](https://ref.ly/Gal3:13); [Ephesians 1:7](https://ref.ly/Eph1:7); [Hebrews 9:15](https://ref.ly/Heb9:15)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 3:40-51
* **Associated Articles:** Numbers 3:11-13 (ID: `22874@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Levi (ID: `person:Levi.3`); Tribe of Levi (ID: `group:Levi`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Shekel (ID: `realia:Shekel`); Gerah (ID: `realia:Gerah`)

## Numbers 4:1 (id: 22974)

Moses and Aaron communicated God's will about the care and transport of the Tent of Meeting. This was the main duty of the three Levitical families.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 4:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`)

## Numbers 4:1–49 (id: 22972)

* This section continues the discussion from [3:21–37](https://ref.ly/Num3:21-Num3:37) about the duties of the three Levite clans. The Levites carried and cared for the Tent of Meeting while traveling and camping. Other tribes always surrounded them to protect them and their valuable cargo ([2:17](https://ref.ly/Num2:17)). They placed the holy items of God in the center of the tribes. The priests and their Levitical assistants kept these items holy by separating them.
* God, in his holiness, deserves to have the details of his will given careful attention. The Tent of Meeting and its furnishings were holy. The tribe of Levi had to maintain the difference between holy and common, and between ceremonially clean and unclean. This is why there is so much information about the duties of the three Levite clans.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 4:1-49

## Numbers 4:3 (id: 22978)

Out of the 22,000 Levites, only men aged thirty to fifty could serve in the sanctuary ([3:39](https://ref.ly/Num3:39)). This was because they were most capable at this time of their lives. [4:34–49](https://ref.ly/Num4:34-Num4:49) lists 8,580 qualified males. This is the summary of this registration, another "numbering."

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 4:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`)

## Numbers 4:4–20 (id: 22979)

The duties of the Kohathites come first because they handled the most holy objects (see [3:27–32](https://ref.ly/Num3:27-Num3:32)). This might be because Aaron and the priests came from this clan ([1 Chronicles 6:2–3](https://ref.ly/1Chr6:2-1Chr6:3)). Their tasks included carrying, setting up, and folding up these holy and very important coverings and furnishings. 

The items entrusted to Kohath's descendants were very valuable. These included: 

* the ark of the Testimony,
* the table of the Bread of the Presence,
* the lampstand (Hebrew *menorah*),
* the gold incense altar, and
* the altar for sacrifices.

[Exodus 25–30](https://ref.ly/Exod25:1-Exod30:38) first describes these objects. But Numbers shows how the Hebrews used them in daily life. The Kohathites carried these items on poles or in frames. They could not touch them or enter the sanctuary to look at them, or they would die.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 4:4-20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Purple Cloth (ID: `realia:PurpleCloth`); Leather (ID: `realia:Leather`); Dugong (ID: `fauna:Dugong`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Covering (ID: `realia:Covering`); Kohath (ID: `person:Kohath`); Tabernacle Altar (ID: `realia:TabernacleAltar`); Ark of the Covenant (ID: `realia:ArkOfTheCovenant`); Temple Altar (ID: `realia:TempleAltar`); Stone Altar (ID: `realia:StoneAltar`); Altar (ID: `realia:Altar`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Shovel (ID: `realia:Shovel`); Olive Oil (ID: `realia:OliveOil`); Anointing Oil (ID: `realia:AnointingOil`); Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`); Levi (ID: `person:Levi.3`); Eleazar (ID: `person:Eleazar.2`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Covenant Box (ID: `keyterm:CovenantBox`); Kohathites (ID: `group:Kohath`); Testimony (ID: `keyterm:Witness.2`); Tribe of Levi (ID: `group:Levi`); Dish (ID: `realia:Dish`); Fork (ID: `realia:Fork`); Jug (ID: `realia:Jug`); Wine (ID: `realia:Wine`); Screen (ID: `realia:Screen`); Curtain (ID: `realia:Curtain`); Lampstand (ID: `realia:Lampstand.2`); Lamp (ID: `realia:Lamp`)

## Numbers 4:15 (id: 22973)

Aaron and his sons, Eleazar and Ithamar, had to fully cover the sanctuary and all holy items before the camp could move. Non\-Levitical tribes marched in front and behind the Levites, who carried the Tent of Meeting. The colors of the coverings showed the royalty of the objects. 

They were so holy that even the Kohathites, whom God chose to handle them, could not touch the holy items, or they would die. This respect showed the holiness of Israel's Lord. Ignoring these rules was dangerous, as shown in [1 Samuel 6:19–20](https://ref.ly/1Sam6:19-1Sam6:20) and [2 Samuel 6:6–7](https://ref.ly/2Sam6:6-2Sam6:7).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 4:15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Kohath (ID: `person:Kohath`); Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`)

## Numbers 4:21–28 (id: 22975)

The men of the Gershonite clan (see [3:21–26](https://ref.ly/Num3:21-Num3:26)) were responsible for general service and carrying loads ([4:24](https://ref.ly/Num4:24)). They handled the curtains, coverings, ropes, and related equipment. These were less central to Israel's worship. The duties of the Gershonites focused on the Tent of Meeting in general. This was unlike the Kohathites, who were responsible for the holy objects of the inner place of worship.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 4:21-28

This assignment to the Merarite clan involved items of lower priority than those of the Kohathites and Gershonites. It had to be done in a specific way, with each man's load assigned by name ([4:32](https://ref.ly/Num4:32); see also [1 Corinthians 14:40](https://ref.ly/1Cor14:40)). The Gershonites and Merarites did not have the status of Kohath’s family. Yet, each group helped move and set up the Tent of Meeting.

## Numbers 5:1–4 (id: 22982)

* Modern Western people may find it difficult to understand the Old Testament laws of purity. Today, things like skin diseases, bodily discharges, or contact with a dead body are viewed mainly as health concerns. But in the Old Testament, the main concern was protecting God’s holiness from ceremonial uncleanness (see [Leviticus 11–15](https://ref.ly/Lev11:1-Lev15:33)).

    The community had to keep the camp holy. Nothing unclean could touch what belonged to the Lord ([Numbers 5:2–3](https://ref.ly/Num5:2-Num5:3)). They had to choose between having God in the camp or allowing a ceremonially polluted person stay. Both could not remain ([5:3](https://ref.ly/Num5:3)).

* The importance of purity continues from Leviticus and Numbers to the book of Revelation. Nothing ceremonially unclean will enter the New Jerusalem where God lives (see study note on Revelation 21:27). In the New Testament, impurity most often refers to what is morally impure ([Acts 10:28](https://ref.ly/Acts10:28); [Ephesians 5:5](https://ref.ly/Eph5:5); [1 Thessalonians 2:3](https://ref.ly/1Thess2:3); [4:7](https://ref.ly/1Thess4:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 5:1-4
* **Associated Articles:** Revelation 21:27 (ID: `25406@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Defilement (ID: `keyterm:Defilement`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Infectious Skin Disease (ID: `keyterm:InfectiousSkinDisease`)

## Numbers 5:1–10.10 (id: 22980)

These legal matters were meant to protect the purity of the Israelites, their priests, and the tabernacle. Purity here refers to being clean and set apart for worship.

These rules helped the people remember who they were. Israel was a theocracy (a nation ruled directly by God). God was their true king.

Moses served as God’s main spokesman. Through him, God gave laws and instructions to the people.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 5:1-31

## Numbers 5:2 (id: 22989)

* *skin disease:* The Hebrew word here describes various, contagious skin diseases (compare [Leviticus 13–14](https://ref.ly/Lev13:1-Lev14:57)).
* *bodily discharge*: This refers to fluids associated with sexual organs ([Leviticus 15](https://ref.ly/Lev15:1-Lev15:33); compare [Luke 8:43–48](https://ref.ly/Luke8:43-Luke8:48)).
* *a dead person:* See [Numbers 6:6–11](https://ref.ly/Num6:6-Num6:11); [19:11](https://ref.ly/Num19:11).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 5:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Defilement (ID: `keyterm:Defilement`); Infectious Skin Disease (ID: `keyterm:InfectiousSkinDisease`)

## Numbers 5:5–10 (id: 22990)

The laws about confession and restitution show the strong Hebrew concern for moral behavior ([Leviticus 6:1–7](https://ref.ly/Lev6:1-Lev6:7)).

Confession means admitting one’s sin before God. Restitution means making things right by repaying what was taken or damaged. A person who wronged another had to return what was stolen and often add more as a penalty.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 5:5-10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Appease (ID: `keyterm:Appease`); Atonement (ID: `keyterm:Atonement`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Repent (ID: `keyterm:Repent`); Be Unfaithful (ID: `keyterm:Unfaithful`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`)

## Numbers 5:6 (id: 22991)

Harming another person is also betraying the Lord (literally *to* *act unfaithful in unfaithfulness*; compare [Leviticus 5:8](https://ref.ly/Num5:8)). Being right with God leads to being right with others. If we treat others wrongly, it shows our relationship with the Lord is not right.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 5:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Be Unfaithful (ID: `keyterm:Unfaithful`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`)

## Numbers 5:7 (id: 22992)

This restitution and the guilt offering for the sin were intended to teach personal responsibility to live holy with God (see [Leviticus 6–7](https://ref.ly/Lev6:1-Lev7:38)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 5:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Repent (ID: `keyterm:Repent`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`)

## Numbers 5:8 (id: 22993)

* Since sin is ultimately against God, the one doing wrong must make restitution to the *LORD* ([Leviticus 5:6](https://ref.ly/Num5:6); [Psalm 51:4](https://ref.ly/Ps51:4)). God allowed the human victims of the sin to receive this payment. If no victims were alive, the priest would receive the payment on behalf of the LORD. Those doing wrong must also offer a valuable sacrifice to restore their ceremonial purity and their relationship with God ([Numbers 5:1–4](https://ref.ly/Num5:1-Num5:4)).
* *the atonement is made for him:* See study note on Leviticus 1:4.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 5:8
* **Associated Articles:** Leviticus 1:4 (ID: `20073@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Appease (ID: `keyterm:Appease`); Atonement (ID: `keyterm:Atonement`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`)

## Numbers 5:9–10 (id: 22994)

Priests partially supported their families by receiving a share of the sacrifices. Sometimes, they could sell extra sacrifices to fund the priesthood, maintain the sanctuary, and help with other religious needs (see [1 Corinthians 8:4–13](https://ref.ly/1Cor8:4-1Cor8:13)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 5:9-10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`)

## Numbers 5:11–31 (id: 22983)

This detailed test for adultery, a "trial by ordeal," is the only one in the Old Testament. Yet, trial by ordeal was common in the ancient Near East. God had a special interest in protecting marriage. It was the foundation of Hebrew society. 

The ordeal was intended to promote marital faithfulness. We do not know how often such ordeals actually happened. This procedure relied on God to intervene so that justice occurred when evidence was lacking (see [1 Corinthians 5:5](https://ref.ly/1Cor5:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 5:11-31

## Numbers 5:29–31 (id: 22988)

The law describes a trial by ordeal (a test in which God was asked to show guilt or innocence). In this case, a husband could bring his wife to the priest if he suspected adultery.

The summary of the law makes one point clear. If the husband raised questions about his wife’s guilt or innocence, he was not considered guilty simply for asking.

Some readers may notice what seems like a double standard. Ancient laws often focused on the woman’s failure in cases of adultery. This pattern was common in the ancient world.

However, Israel’s legal system did not excuse the man involved. The law required punishment for both parties in an affair (as in [Leviticus 20:10](https://ref.ly/Lev20:10); [Deuteronomy 22:21–22](https://ref.ly/Deut22:21-Deut22:22)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 5:29-31

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); Instruction (ID: `keyterm:Instruction`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Be Blameless (ID: `keyterm:Blameless`); Defilement (ID: `keyterm:Defilement`); Bear the Consequences Of (ID: `keyterm:BearTheConsequencesOf`); Be Zealous (ID: `keyterm:Zealous`)

## Numbers 6:1–21 (id: 22995)

The voluntary *vow of a Nazirite* allowed men or women to *separate* themselves *to the LORD*. The Nazirite lifestyle varied throughout Hebrew history. People took vows seriously in ancient times as a way to show devotion or gratitude to God. This vow could be temporary or permanent. It involved avoiding alcoholic drinks, not cutting hair, and avoiding contact with corpses. 

Nazirite restrictions allowed members of non\-Levitical tribes to have a more demanding and highly respected relationship with God. The Nazirite vow is probably best known because of Samson ([Judges 13–16](https://ref.ly/Judg13:1-Judg16:31)). See also [1 Samuel 1:11](https://ref.ly/1Sam1:11); [Jeremiah 35](https://ref.ly/Jer35:1-Jer35:19); [Acts 18:18](https://ref.ly/Acts18:18); [21:23–26](https://ref.ly/Acts21:23-Acts21:26); compare [Luke 1:15](https://ref.ly/Luke1:15).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 18:18; Numbers 6:1-21

The law required priests to avoid drinking wine before serving in the Tent of Meeting ([Leviticus 10:9](https://ref.ly/Lev10:9)). The Nazirite vow allowed non\-Levites to extend this rule. They avoided wine everywhere, at all times, and anything from a grapevine.

## Numbers 6:5 (id: 23004)

*hair:* This had a special symbolic meaning (see [Leviticus 19:27](https://ref.ly/Lev19:27)). Religious people often left their hair uncut or shaved it completely ([Numbers 8:5–7](https://ref.ly/Num8:5-Num8:7)). The Israelites did not have to leave their hair completely uncut, but the Nazirites did.

## Numbers 6:6–8 (id: 23005)

Touching dead bodies made people ceremonially unclean ([9:6–10](https://ref.ly/Num9:6-Num9:10)). Priests could only touch the dead bodies of close relatives ([Leviticus 21:1–4](https://ref.ly/Lev21:1-Lev21:4)). But Nazirites could not touch any dead body at all.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 6:6-8

## Numbers 6:9–21 (id: 23006)

The law gave instructions for restoring purity after unavoidable contact with a dead body. Contact with a dead body made a person ceremonially unclean (not fit for worship for a period of time). The person had to follow certain steps in order to be restored.

Special procedures and offerings also marked the end of a Nazirite vow. A Nazirite vow was a special promise to set oneself apart for God for a period of time. When the time ended, the person brought sacrifices and completed the required rituals.

The law also gives a strong warning about fulfilling the vow ([6:21](https://ref.ly/Num6:21)). A person who made such a promise had to complete it fully. God expected faithfulness in keeping vows made to him.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 6:9-21

## Numbers 6:22–27 (id: 22996)

The priestly blessing connects to the themes of worship and purity in chapters [5–6](https://ref.ly/Num5:1-Num6:27). It is a prayer where priests ask for God's blessings for Israel. As representatives of God, priests had the duty to pray (compare [Leviticus 9:23](https://ref.ly/Lev9:23); [Deuteronomy 10:8](https://ref.ly/Deut10:8)). This prayer is similar to the high priestly prayer of Jesus in [John 17](https://ref.ly/John17:1-John17:26).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 6:22-27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); To Be Kindly Disposed (ID: `keyterm:KindlyDisposed`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`)

## Numbers 6:24 (id: 22998)

The first half of this verse asks for blessings like health, wealth, and fertility. 

The second half seeks protection from all kinds of dangers. This prayer is similar to the language in [Psalms 121:3–8](https://ref.ly/Ps121:3-Ps121:8); [140:4](https://ref.ly/Ps140:4); [and 141:9](https://ref.ly/Ps141:9). These sincere requests are still good examples for prayer and blessings that people can seek for everyone. The entire poem emphasizes the Lord as the source of all good things.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 6:24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Numbers 6:24–26 (id: 22997)

[Psalm 67:1](https://ref.ly/Ps67:1) has a short version of this blessing. This is similar to one found on a silver amulet from Ketef Hinnom, southwest of Jerusalem’s Old City. A second, larger silver amulet from the same tomb has the full blessing with words almost the same as here. 

The Second Temple period lasted from Ezra to the time of Jesus. Priests would give a blessing at each day's sacrifice or at the end of services. This blessing is still used in Jewish and Christian liturgy (the formal order of prayers and worship, as in [6:27](https://ref.ly/Num6:27)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 6:24-26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); To Be Kindly Disposed (ID: `keyterm:KindlyDisposed`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`)

## Numbers 6:25 (id: 22999)

* *may the LORD cause His face to shine upon you*: This is a desire for God's approval*.*
* *be gracious to you:* God's people need his grace and mercy in a dangerous world (compare [Psalms 31:16](https://ref.ly/Ps31:16); [67:1](https://ref.ly/Ps67:1); [80:3](https://ref.ly/Ps80:3), [7](https://ref.ly/Ps80:7), [19](https://ref.ly/Ps80:19)).

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Be Kindly Disposed (ID: `keyterm:KindlyDisposed`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Numbers 6:26 (id: 23000)

God's favor, shown through his countenance or face, means his approval and special attention (compare [Psalm 30:7](https://ref.ly/Ps30:7)). Divine peace (Hebrew *shalom*) is more than only the absence of conflict or violence. God's peace brings complete well\-being, health, and wholeness.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 6:26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Numbers 6:27 (id: 23001)

The priests were mediators of God’s covenant with Israel. A mediator is someone who stands between two parties to represent them. The priests stood between God and the people.

Their duty was to pray for the people. They also spoke God’s blessing over them. In this way, they helped maintain the covenant relationship between God and Israel (see [Romans 8:34](https://ref.ly/Rom8:34); [Hebrews 2:17–18](https://ref.ly/Heb2:17-Heb2:18); [4:14–16](https://ref.ly/Heb4:14-Heb4:16)). He intercedes for believers, which means he prays for them before God. As the great high priest, he brings his people into God’s presence and secures God’s blessing for them.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 6:27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)

## Numbers 7:10–11 (id: 23008)

When they anointed the altar, the tribal leaders gave the wagons and oxen, silver and gold vessels, and other religious offerings for the altar. Starting with the tribe of Judah, the leaders brought these gifts to the tabernacle for 12 days. They collected these gifts from the Egyptians when Israel left Egypt (compare [Exodus 12:35–36](https://ref.ly/Exod12:35-Exod12:36); [25:3](https://ref.ly/Exod25:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 7:10-11

## Numbers 7:12–83 (id: 23009)

One by one, the tribal leaders brought their gifts for the dedication of the altar. Each tribe participated until the whole Hebrew confederation (the united group of twelve tribes) had taken part.

The text repeats the description of each tribe’s gift. This repetition highlights the shared nature of the ceremony. Every tribe had a role in setting apart their place of worship for God.

The careful listing of the gifts may reflect detailed record keeping, like an archive or ledger (an official written record). The text notes that the silver bowls and plates contained grain offerings. The gold dishes held incense.

Each tribe also brought animals for a burnt offering, a sin offering, and a peace offering, as required in Leviticus. These offerings showed worship, repentance, and fellowship with God.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 7:12-83

## Numbers 7:84–88 (id: 23012)

This paragraph first summarizes the gifts and offerings from each tribe. Then it lists expensive containers, offerings, incense, and sacrificial animals. These large and costly gifts showed the importance of the altar, the tabernacle, and the God who was served there. The law required these sacrifices, but the Hebrews gave the special gifts generously.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 7:84-88

## Numbers 7:89 (id: 23013)

* After dedicating the Tent of Meeting (the tabernacle) and setting it apart as holy, it became the center of divine revelation. There, Moses spoke with God, who revealed his will and presence ([Leviticus 16:2](https://ref.ly/Lev16:2), [13–17](https://ref.ly/Lev16:13-Lev16:17)). This communication came from the ark of the covenant. It was specifically between the two cherubim above the ark's cover, also called "the mercy seat" (compare [Exodus 25:17–22](https://ref.ly/Exod25:17-Exod25:22)).
* For the Israelites, this was the special place for atonement, similar to the significance in the Christian faith of the cross where Jesus died (see [Hebrews 9–10](https://ref.ly/Heb9:1-Heb10:39), especially [Hebrews 9:5](https://ref.ly/Heb9:5)). God's voice, heard by Moses the mediator, fulfilled the promise in [Exodus 25:22](https://ref.ly/Exod25:22) and [Numbers 30:6](https://ref.ly/Num30:6). The Lord's presence ensured the holiness of Israel's wilderness place of worship.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 7:89

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`); Covenant Box (ID: `keyterm:CovenantBox`); Testimony (ID: `keyterm:Witness.2`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Cover Lid (ID: `realia:CoverLid`); Winged Creature (ID: `realia:WingedCreature`); Ark of the Covenant (ID: `realia:ArkOfTheCovenant`)

## Numbers 8:1–4 (id: 23015)

These verses give specific instructions for placing the lampstand (Hebrew *menorah*) in the tabernacle. The lampstand (see [Exodus 25:31–40](https://ref.ly/Exod25:31-Exod25:40)). It was made with branches shaped like a stylized tree.

The inside of the tabernacle had no windows, so it was dark. The light from the lampstand helped the priests carry out their duties.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 8:1-4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Lampstand (ID: `realia:Lampstand.2`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Lamp (ID: `realia:Lamp`); Hammer (ID: `realia:Hammer`)

## Numbers 8:5–22 (id: 23021)

The Lord gave instructions for the dedication and cleansing of the Levites. The Levites were set apart to serve in the tabernacle.

## Numbers 8:7 (id: 23022)

* The ceremony to make the Levites ceremonially clean involved sprinkling with water, shaving all hair, and wearing clean clothes. This was similar to ceremonies for cleansing from ceremonial impurity.
* *water of purification:* This phrase appears only here. The sprinkling symbolized washing away sin and had symbolic importance similar to later Jewish washings and Christian baptism. This water might have been the same as the mixture with ashes from the red heifer that removed sin (see [19:9](https://ref.ly/Num19:9); also [Hebrews 9:13](https://ref.ly/Heb9:13)).
* Shaving their entire body was also part of being declared ceremonially clean from the ceremonial impurity of polluting diseases ([Leviticus 14:8](https://ref.ly/Lev14:8)).
* Washing clothes was part of all cleansing from ceremonial impurity (for example, [Leviticus 15:5–27](https://ref.ly/Lev15:5-Lev15:27)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 8:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pure (ID: `keyterm:Pure`); To Cleanse (ID: `keyterm:Cleanse.2`); Razor (ID: `realia:Razor`)

## Numbers 8:23–26 (id: 23020)

* These are guidelines for the length of the Levites' service.
* Whereas [4:3](https://ref.ly/Num4:3) says their service in the Tent of Meeting started at age 30, [8:24](https://ref.ly/Num8:24) says age 25\. This difference might show changes in age requirements over time, depending on how many Levite men were available. Those aged 25–29 might have been unofficial workers or apprentices.
* Both passages set the retirement age at 50\. Retired Levites could serve as guards of the Tent of Meeting (see [Psalm 84:10](https://ref.ly/Ps84:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 8:23-26

## Numbers 9:13 (id: 23025)

If a person refused to observe the required Passover, that person was to be cut off from the community. The Passover was a central act of worship that remembered God’s rescue of Israel from Egypt.

The phrase “cut off” can have more than one meaning in the Old Testament. It may refer to the death penalty. It may also mean banishment from the community (being removed from the people). The exact meaning in this case is not fully clear (see study note on Leviticus 7:20–21).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 9:13
* **Associated Articles:** Leviticus 7:20-21 (ID: `20386@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

## Numbers 10:1–10 (id: 22608)

* Israel needed clear signals so the tribes could receive instructions. Two silver trumpets provided a simple and effective way to signal all the tribes to move.
* These trumpets were not the ram’s horns (Hebrew *shofar*) often mentioned in the Old Testament (for example, [Joshua 6:4–6](https://ref.ly/Josh6:4-Josh6:6)). Israelite craftsmen made them from hammered silver brought out of Egypt.

## Numbers 10:11–36 (id: 22609)

Leaving Sinai was a key moment in Israel's history. The Israelites now had the law and God would test them on how they obeyed it. The departure from Sinai marked the start of the march toward Canaan, although it became a long journey (compare [Deuteronomy 1:6–8](https://ref.ly/Deut1:6-Deut1:8), [19](https://ref.ly/Deut1:19)). This passage summarizes the daily routines during the journey.

## Numbers 10:12 (id: 22610)

The Israelites traveled to the wilderness of Paran. This region was dry and harsh. It was either arid or semi\-arid, which means it received very little rain.

The wilderness of Paran lay in the northeastern part of the Sinai Peninsula. It was south of the Negev and west of the Dead Sea and the Arabah Rift (a long valley that runs from the Dead Sea southward; compare [Genesis 21:21](https://ref.ly/Gen21:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 10:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Wilderness of Sinai (ID: `place:SinaiWilderness`); Paran (ID: `place:Paran`)

## Numbers 10:13–28 (id: 22611)

The order of march described here is different from [2:3–34](https://ref.ly/Num2:3-Num2:34). There, all three Levitical clans carried the tabernacle and its furnishings in the center of the camp. They marched between the groups led by Judah and Reuben, and the groups led by Ephraim and Dan.

In this passage, the arrangement is more detailed. The Gershonites and Merarites carried the structural parts of the tabernacle between Judah and Reuben. The Kohathites carried the most sacred furniture between Reuben and Ephraim.

This arrangement was practical. It gave the Gershonites and Merarites time to set up the tabernacle before the Kohathites arrived with the sacred objects. This plan also provided protection for the holy items.

In addition, this pattern spread the Levites among the tribes. Their presence among the people may have strengthened spiritual influence throughout the camp (compare [Matthew 13:33](https://ref.ly/Matt13:33); [16:6](https://ref.ly/Matt16:6), [11–12](https://ref.ly/Matt16:11-Matt16:12); [1 Corinthians 5:6–7](https://ref.ly/1Cor5:6-1Cor5:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 10:13-28

## Numbers 10:29–32 (id: 22613)

* After the journey toward Paran began, Moses asked his brother\-in\-law, Hobab, to guide the Israelites (literally *and you will serve for us for eyes*) (compare [Judges 4:11](https://ref.ly/Judg4:11)). In this way, Hobab would share in their promised blessings. As a Midianite shepherd, Hobab knew the routes and possible sources of food and water in the Sinai Peninsula.
* *Reuel the Midianite:* See “Jethro (Reuel)” Profile.
* *my own land and my own people:* Midian was probably in northwestern Arabia. [Judges 1:16](https://ref.ly/Judg1:16) suggests that Hobab accepted the request.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 10:29-32
* **Associated Articles:** Jethro (Reuel) (ID: `500469@TyndaleStudyNotesProfiles`)

## Numbers 10:33 (id: 22614)

## Numbers 11:1 (id: 22623)

* *Soon the people began to complain …* *fire from the LORD blazed:* The link between Israel’s complaints and God’s punishment is a major theme in Numbers and throughout the Old Testament (for example, [Deuteronomy 9:22](https://ref.ly/Deut9:22); [Psalm 78](https://ref.ly/Ps78:1-Ps78:72)). God is holy and just, and he demands obedience from his people. Complaints are the same as rejecting him (compare [Numbers 11:20](https://ref.ly/Num11:20)).
* *their hardship:* Mount Sinai had been a stable home for a year. The journey to Paran brought heat, thirst, and fatigue.
* *consumed the outskirts of the camp**:* Compare [Leviticus 10:2](https://ref.ly/Lev10:2).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 11:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Numbers 11:1–35 (id: 22620)

## Numbers 11:2–3 (id: 22624)

* The people asked Moses to speak to God again on their behalf. This stopped the fire of God's judgment (compare [Exodus 15:25](https://ref.ly/Exod15:25); [32:11–14](https://ref.ly/Exod32:11-Exod32:14)).
* *Taberah:* Compare [Numbers 11:34–35](https://ref.ly/Num11:34-Num11:35); see [Deuteronomy 9:22](https://ref.ly/Deut9:22).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 11:2-3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Taberah (ID: `place:Taberah`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`)

## Numbers 11:4–6 (id: 22630)

*Who will feed us meat?:* The Israelites had flocks and herds (for example, [32:1](https://ref.ly/Num32:1); [Exodus 12:32](https://ref.ly/Exod12:32), [38](https://ref.ly/Exod12:38); [17:3](https://ref.ly/Exod17:3); [34:3](https://ref.ly/Exod34:3)). But the animals were more valuable for their dairy products and other benefits while alive. They only butchered them for meat occasionally. The Hebrews did not have enough meat to regularly feed such a large population (compare [Numbers 11:21–22](https://ref.ly/Num11:21-Num11:22)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 11:4-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Onion (ID: `flora:Onion`); Watermelon (ID: `flora:Watermelon`); Muskmelon (ID: `flora:Muskmelon`); Leek (ID: `flora:Leek`); Garlic (ID: `flora:Garlic`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Fish (ID: `fauna:Fish`)

## Numbers 11:4–15 (id: 22629)

Israel had not given up the attitude that led to judgment at Taberah ([11:1–3](https://ref.ly/Num11:1-Num11:3)). At Taberah, the people complained, and the Lord sent fire among them.

Soon after, they joined the foreign rabble (a mixed group of non\-Israelites who had left Egypt with them) in craving the foods of Egypt. They longed for the fish, vegetables, and other foods they had known there.

In doing this, they forgot the suffering and slavery they had experienced in Egypt. They remembered the food but ignored the hardship.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 11:4-15

People often explain *manna* as a naturally occurring edible substance (but see[Deuteronomy 8:3](https://ref.ly/Deut8:3)). Yet, its amount and how long it lasted was miraculous.

## Numbers 11:10–15 (id: 22621)

* The Israelites' constant complaining frustrated Moses, leading him to express his own complaints to God. With extreme sarcasm, Moses reminded God that he had not wanted this responsibility (compare [Exodus 4:10](https://ref.ly/Exod4:10)). Israel became such a burden that Moses wished God would do him the favor of ending his life (compare [Job 6:9](https://ref.ly/Job6:9)).
* Moses' complaint was acceptable, unlike the people's complaints, because he sought the Lord instead of longing for Egypt's pleasures.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 11:10-15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Swear (ID: `keyterm:Swear`); Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`)

## Numbers 11:16–30 (id: 22622)

Jethro suggested that Moses should have different levels of judges ([Exodus 18:13–26](https://ref.ly/Exod18:13-Exod18:26)). He focused on practical issues like the tribal leaders who helped Moses with the registration ([Numbers 1:5–15](https://ref.ly/Num1:5-Num1:15)). 

In contrast, the group of 70 elders would give Israel spiritual guidance. They needed a share of God's Spirit. Moses already had this, and it made him the Lord's special spokesman. Their ability to lead depended on God's Spirit being with them (compare [1 Samuel 10:6](https://ref.ly/1Sam10:6); [19:20](https://ref.ly/1Sam19:20); [Joel 2:28](https://ref.ly/Joel2:28); [Acts 2:16–18](https://ref.ly/Acts2:16-Acts2:18); [1 Corinthians 12:10](https://ref.ly/1Cor12:10); [2 Peter 1:20–21](https://ref.ly/2Pet1:20-2Pet1:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 11:16-30

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Speak as a Prophet (ID: `keyterm:Speak`); Medad (ID: `person:Medad`); Eldad (ID: `person:Eldad`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`); Be Zealous (ID: `keyterm:Zealous`); Joshua (ID: `person:Joshua.2`); Nun (ID: `person:Nun`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`); Fish (ID: `fauna:Fish`)

## Numbers 11:25 (id: 22625)

The text does not describe what happened when the Spirit rested on them and they prophesied. It only says that they spoke as the Spirit enabled them.

Whatever took place, it may have included ecstatic speech (intense or Spirit\-driven words spoken in a special way). Similar events appear later in [Acts 2:4](https://ref.ly/Acts2:4) and [1 Corinthians 14:2](https://ref.ly/1Cor14:2). However, the text suggests that this was a unique event.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 11:25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Speak as a Prophet (ID: `keyterm:Speak`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Numbers 11:28–29 (id: 22626)

Joshua’s worthy jealousy was a desire to safeguard his master’s status. But, Moses was humble and did not feel threatened by Eldad and Medad's prophecy ([Numbers 12:3](https://ref.ly/Num12:3)). Instead, he was glad to see God working through others in special ways. Moses had leadership qualities that the younger Joshua had not yet developed (compare [Mark 9:38–40](https://ref.ly/Mark9:38-Mark9:40)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 11:28-29

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Be Zealous (ID: `keyterm:Zealous`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Joshua (ID: `person:Joshua.2`); Nun (ID: `person:Nun`)

God answered the people's complaints by sending a storm of quail and a plague. God had given quail with manna in [Exodus 16:13](https://ref.ly/Exod16:13). But here the quail showed God's judgment through an excessive abundance.

## Numbers 12:1 (id: 22637)

Most interpreters believe *the Cushite woman* is Zipporah, the wife of Moses. She was from Midian ([Exodus 2:16–22](https://ref.ly/Exod2:16-Exod2:22)). They see *Cush* as referring to Cushan, a region and people near Midian ([Habakkuk 3:7](https://ref.ly/Hab3:7)). 

Other interpreters think *Cushite* refers to Kusi in northern Arabia, Ethiopia, or Sudan. This would suggest she was a different wife. 

 In either case, it is possible that Miriam and Aaron disapproved of his having married a non\-Israelite or that they were jealous of her influence over Moses.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 12:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Cushites (ID: `group:Cush`); Miriam (ID: `person:Miriam`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Cush (ID: `place:Cush.2`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`)

## Numbers 12:1–2 (id: 22634)

Miriam and Aaron may have seen the marriage of Moses as a threat to their status and goals. The verses list Miriam first, suggesting she started this family conflict. This could explain why God punished her and not Aaron ([12:9–10](https://ref.ly/Num12:9-Num12:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 12:1-2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Cushites (ID: `group:Cush`); Miriam (ID: `person:Miriam`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Cush (ID: `place:Cush.2`)

## Numbers 12:1–16 (id: 22633)

Rebellion did not come only from the “rabble” or from the Israelites who were tired of manna ([11:4](https://ref.ly/Num11:4)). It also came from within the family of Moses.

 Aaron the high priest and Miriam the prophetess spoke against Moses ([Exodus 29:30](https://ref.ly/Exod29:30); [Exodus 15:20](https://ref.ly/Exod15:20)). Their actions showed that even Israel’s spiritual leaders struggled with jealousy and doubt.

At this time, Israel was moving toward a lowest point in its rebellion against God ([Numbers 13:25–14:12](https://ref.ly/Num13:25-Num14:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 12:1-16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Miriam (ID: `person:Miriam`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`); Cushites (ID: `group:Cush`); Vision (ID: `keyterm:Vision`); Paran (ID: `place:Paran`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Infectious Skin Disease (ID: `keyterm:InfectiousSkinDisease`); Cush (ID: `place:Cush.2`); Well-Established (ID: `keyterm:BeFirm`); Hazeroth (ID: `place:Hazeroth`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`)

## Numbers 12:2 (id: 22638)

* Challenging the authority of Moses meant rejecting the Lord's choice of him as Israel's leader.
* *the LORD heard this:* The same phrase in [11:1](https://ref.ly/Num11:1) hints at the Lord's response.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 12:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`)

## Numbers 12:3 (id: 22639)

* The humility of Moses stands in sharp contrast to the jealousy of Miriam and Aaron. The text says that Moses was very humble. His character reflected a godly spirit (see also [Proverbs 3:34](https://ref.ly/Prov3:34); [Matthew 5:5](https://ref.ly/Matt5:5)).
* The statement about the humility of Moses appears as a parenthetical comment (a brief note inserted into the story). It presents the truth from God’s point of view.

    Some think these words may have been written by Moses. Others believe they may have been added later by an editor. In either case, the comment highlights the faithful and humble character of Moses.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 12:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`)

In response to this rebellion, the Lord spoke directly. He reaffirmed the special status and authority of Moses.

## Numbers 13:1–14.45 (id: 22643)

Twelve spies explored the fertile land of Canaan and found strong enemies defending it. Earlier chapters show Israel's disobedience, hinting at future failures. The Israelites did not have the obedience, courage, or faith to capture the land. So, the Lord made them spend 40 years in the Wilderness of Sinai. That generation did not receive their inheritance in the promised land.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 13:1-14:38

## Numbers 13:21 (id: 22648)

* The distance from the Wilderness of Zin to Lebo\-hamath in Syria was about 275 miles. The Wilderness of Zin was between the wilderness of Paran and Canaan's southern border see [34:3–5](https://ref.ly/Num34:3-Num34:5); [Joshua 15:1–4](https://ref.ly/Josh15:1-Josh15:4)). *Lebo\-hamath* means "entrance of Hamath." This could refer to a mountain pass or a city near Hamath.
* The exact location of Rehob is unclear. But it was probably the same as Old Testament Beth\-rehob, near a major route to Hamath. This text outlines the approximate borders of the promised land (see [1 Kings 8:65](https://ref.ly/1Kgs8:65)). The phrase *from Dan to Beersheba* often marked Israel’s traditional boundaries ([Judges 20:1](https://ref.ly/Judg20:1); [2 Samuel 3:10](https://ref.ly/2Sam3:10)). It covered a smaller area than the territory explored by Israel’s spies.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 13:21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Beth-rehob (ID: `place:Beth-rehob`); Wilderness of Zin (ID: `place:Zin`); Hamath (ID: `place:Hamath`)

## Numbers 13:21–24 (id: 22647)

The spies explored the land of Canaan. In contrast to the Sinai wilderness, it was rich and fertile.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 13:21-24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Valley of Eshcol (ID: `place:EshcolValley`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`); Beth-rehob (ID: `place:Beth-rehob`); Anak (ID: `person:Anak`); Talmai (ID: `person:Talmai`); Zoan (ID: `place:Zoan`); Ahiman (ID: `person:Ahiman`); Sheshai (ID: `person:Sheshai`); Hebron (ID: `place:Hebron`); Hamath (ID: `place:Hamath`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Wilderness of Zin (ID: `place:Zin`); Negev (ID: `place:Negeb`); Fig (ID: `flora:Fig`); Pomegranate (ID: `flora:Pomegranate`)

## Numbers 13:22 (id: 22649)

* Israel's spies traveled into Canaan's hill country through the Negev, the dry southern region near the wilderness of Zin. In the southern part of Canaan's central hill country, they reached Hebron. This was where Israel's ancestors had lived ([Genesis 13:18](https://ref.ly/Gen13:18); [23:19](https://ref.ly/Gen23:19); [35:27](https://ref.ly/Gen35:27); [37:14](https://ref.ly/Gen37:14)).
* The descendants of Anak were famous for their size and strength (for example, [Deuteronomy 9:2](https://ref.ly/Deut9:2)).
* *Zoan* (later called *Tanis* by the Greeks) was a famous city in Egypt's eastern delta (see also [Psalms 78:12](https://ref.ly/Ps78:12), [43](https://ref.ly/Ps78:43); [Isaiah 19:11–13](https://ref.ly/Isa19:11-Isa19:13); [30:3–5](https://ref.ly/Isa30:3-Isa30:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 13:22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Anak (ID: `person:Anak`); Ahiman (ID: `person:Ahiman`); Zoan (ID: `place:Zoan`); Talmai (ID: `person:Talmai`); Sheshai (ID: `person:Sheshai`); Hebron (ID: `place:Hebron`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Negev (ID: `place:Negeb`)

## Numbers 13:25–29 (id: 22650)

The spies agreed the land was good, but most doubted Israel could conquer it. The strong and well\-fed Canaanites had built fortified towns for protection.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 13:25-29

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Wheat (ID: `flora:Wheat`); Sorghum (ID: `flora:Sorghum`); Kadesh-barnea (ID: `place:Kadesh-barnea`); Amalek (ID: `place:Amalek`); Heth (ID: `person:Heth`); Paran (ID: `place:Paran`); Land Flowing of Milk and Honey (ID: `keyterm:LandFlowingOfMilkAndHoney`); Negev (ID: `place:Negeb`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Canaanites (ID: `group:Canaan`); Jordan River (ID: `place:Jordan`); Hittites (ID: `group:Hittite`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`); Amalekites (ID: `group:Amalek`); Amorites (ID: `group:Amorites`); Anak (ID: `person:Anak`); Jebusites (ID: `group:Jebusites`)

## Numbers 13:26 (id: 22651)

Kadesh is often linked to ‘Ain el\-Qudeirat, a spring at the northern border of Sinai and the southern edge of Canaan. There is no evidence of a Hebrew camp there during the exodus or the wilderness wanderings. So, some scholars have suggested that ancient Kadesh was in a different location.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 13:26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kadesh-barnea (ID: `place:Kadesh-barnea`); Paran (ID: `place:Paran`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Wheat (ID: `flora:Wheat`); Sorghum (ID: `flora:Sorghum`)

## Numbers 13:27 (id: 22652)

The promised land is also known as land *flowing with milk and honey*. In biblical times, *milk* and dairy were key parts of the diet. *Milk* symbolized abundance (see [Deuteronomy 32:14](https://ref.ly/Deut32:14); [Isaiah 55:1](https://ref.ly/Isa55:1); [Joel 3:18](https://ref.ly/Joel3:18)). *Honey* added sweetness (see [Psalms 19:10](https://ref.ly/Ps19:10); [119:103](https://ref.ly/Ps119:103); [Ezekiel 3:3](https://ref.ly/Ezek3:3); [Revelation 10:9–10](https://ref.ly/Rev10:9-Rev10:10)). The phrase describes a desirable place with plenty of food and resources (compare [Numbers 16:13–14](https://ref.ly/Num16:13-Num16:14); see also [Exodus 3:8](https://ref.ly/Exod3:8); [Deuteronomy 6:3](https://ref.ly/Deut6:3); [26:15](https://ref.ly/Deut26:15); [Jeremiah 11:4–5](https://ref.ly/Jer11:4-Jer11:5); [Ezekiel 20:6](https://ref.ly/Ezek20:6)). 

The New Testament emphasizes the spiritual nature of God's promises, pointing to a promised inheritance in heaven (see [Romans 9:6–8](https://ref.ly/Rom9:6-Rom9:8); [Galatians 6:14–16](https://ref.ly/Gal6:14-Gal6:16); [Revelation 21:1–2](https://ref.ly/Rev21:1-Rev21:2)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 13:27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Land Flowing of Milk and Honey (ID: `keyterm:LandFlowingOfMilkAndHoney`); Sorghum (ID: `flora:Sorghum`); Wheat (ID: `flora:Wheat`)

## Numbers 14:1–45 (id: 22656)

The people rejected the testimony of the faithful spies, Joshua and Caleb. Then rebellion spread throughout the community. Moses intervened to save Israel from total destruction. Those who disobeyed God died during a 40\-year journey in the wilderness.

## Numbers 14:3–4 (id: 22667)

* The threats they complained about were real, but the Israelites should have trusted the Lord. In the ancient Near East, victors often took wives and children as plunder after battles.
* *go back to Egypt:* The Israelites quickly forgot their slavery and the difficulties of the wilderness ([Exodus 2:23–24](https://ref.ly/Exod2:23-Exod2:24); [Numbers 11:1](https://ref.ly/Num11:1)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 14:3-4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Numbers 14:10 (id: 22657)

*Joshua* *and Caleb* encouraged trust in God, which led to more opposition. The protests against the leaders turned into threats against the faithful spies. All the Israelites' objections stopped suddenly. This was because *the glory* (Hebrew *kabod*) *of the LORD* appeared at the Tent of Meeting, the site of divine communication (see [Exodus 24:15–18](https://ref.ly/Exod24:15-Exod24:18); [Ezekiel 1](https://ref.ly/Ezek1:1-Ezek1:28)) .

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 14:10

## Numbers 14:11 (id: 22658)

God seemed amazed that Israel still did not trust him. This was despite his miraculous rescue from slavery and provision for their needs in the wilderness (see [Deuteronomy 4:32–40](https://ref.ly/Deut4:32-Deut4:40); [Matthew 11:21](https://ref.ly/Matt11:21); [Luke 10:13](https://ref.ly/Luke10:13)).

## Numbers 14:12 (id: 22659)

God is patient, but he will not tolerate open rebellion from his people (see [Exodus 32:7–10](https://ref.ly/Exod32:7-Exod32:10)). 

In the New Testament, the writer of Hebrews mentions this event to strengthen the faith of Christians whose trust in the gospel was weakening ([Hebrews 3:7–19](https://ref.ly/Heb3:7-Heb3:19); [12:3](https://ref.ly/Heb12:3), [25](https://ref.ly/Heb12:25); see also [Psalm 95:8–11](https://ref.ly/Ps95:8-Ps95:11)).

## Numbers 15:1–41 (id: 22677)

Chapters [1–14](https://ref.ly/Num1:1-Num14:45) cover a short time period, but chapter [15](https://ref.ly/Num15:1-Num15:41) marks the start of a long period. This is the main part of the forty years in the wilderness, about which little is known. There are references to events during this time (for example, [Deuteronomy 8:2–6](https://ref.ly/Deut8:2-Deut8:6); [29:5–6](https://ref.ly/Deut29:5-Deut29:6); [Joshua 5:4–8](https://ref.ly/Josh5:4-Josh5:8); [Ezekiel 20:10–26](https://ref.ly/Ezek20:10-Ezek20:26); [Amos 5:25–26](https://ref.ly/Amos5:25-Amos5:26); [Acts 7:42–43](https://ref.ly/Acts7:42-Acts7:43)). 

During this period, Moses continued to give laws to guide Israel’s life as a nation. These laws would be especially important in the future. They were meant for the day when the people who survived the wilderness would enter the promised land. 

This chapter addresses three main types of laws. [Numbers 15](https://ref.ly/Num15:1-Num15:41) is in the middle of stories about a stubborn and disobedient Israel. It offers hope that the promised land still waited for the next generation of Israel’s people.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 15:1-41

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Pleasing Odor (ID: `keyterm:PleasingOdor`); Hin (ID: `realia:Hin`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`); Wine (ID: `realia:Wine`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); Slaughtering (ID: `keyterm:Slaughtering`); Olive Oil (ID: `realia:OliveOil`); Anointing Oil (ID: `realia:AnointingOil`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`); Vow (ID: `keyterm:Vow`); Appease (ID: `keyterm:Appease`); Instruction (ID: `keyterm:Instruction`); Tassel (ID: `realia:Tassel`); Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Play the Prostitute (ID: `keyterm:PlayTheProstitute`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Appointed Feast (ID: `keyterm:AppointedFeast`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); Word of the Lord (ID: `keyterm:WordOfTheLord`); Cake (ID: `realia:Cake`); Threshing Floor (ID: `realia:ThreshingFloor`); Thread (ID: `realia:Thread`); Measuring Reed (ID: `realia:MeasuringReed`); Purple Cloth (ID: `realia:PurpleCloth`)

## Numbers 15:17–21 (id: 22679)

These gifts were signs of gratitude to God for the crops he provided. The people were not to give these offerings right away. They were to begin after the Hebrews reached the promised land.

At that time, they would settle there and begin planting crops. Then they would bring these gifts to thank God for the harvest.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 15:17-21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Cake (ID: `realia:Cake`); Threshing Floor (ID: `realia:ThreshingFloor`)

## Numbers 15:22–29 (id: 22680)

Sins done without knowing were treated differently from sins done on purpose ([15:30–31](https://ref.ly/Num15:30-Num15:31)). The law also made a difference between the actions of the whole community and the actions of one person (see also [Leviticus 4–5](https://ref.ly/Lev4:1-Lev5:19)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 15:22-29

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); Appease (ID: `keyterm:Appease`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`); Pleasing Odor (ID: `keyterm:PleasingOdor`); Instruction (ID: `keyterm:Instruction`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Wine (ID: `realia:Wine`)

## Numbers 15:30–31 (id: 22681)

* Atonement (the way God provides forgiveness and restores a relationship with him) could be made for sins done without knowing or by accident ([15:22–29](https://ref.ly/Num15:22-Num15:29)). However, a person who sinned openly and proudly was treated differently(literally *with a high hand;* compare [Exodus 14:8](https://ref.ly/Exod14:8)). Such a person showed open disrespect toward God. This was considered blasphemy (speaking against God in a dishonoring way). That person had to face serious consequences (compare [Leviticus 24:10–16](https://ref.ly/Lev24:10-Lev24:16); see also [Deuteronomy 17:2–6](https://ref.ly/Deut17:2-Deut17:6); [Hebrews 10:26–31](https://ref.ly/Heb10:26-Heb10:31)).
* *cut off from among his people:* See study notes on Exodus 31:14–15; Leviticus 7:20–21.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 15:30-31
* **Associated Articles:** Exodus 31:14-15 (ID: `11298@TyndaleStudyNotes`); Leviticus 7:20-21 (ID: `20386@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); Word of the Lord (ID: `keyterm:WordOfTheLord`)

## Numbers 15:32–36 (id: 22682)

This story gives an example of what [Numbers 15:30–31](https://ref.ly/Num15:30-Num15:31) teaches. The Sabbath was the Lord’s holy day. The people were not allowed to ignore it or treat it as unimportant ([Exodus 20:8–11](https://ref.ly/Exod20:8-Exod20:11); [31:14–15](https://ref.ly/Exod31:14-Exod31:15); [35:1–3](https://ref.ly/Exod35:1-Exod35:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 15:32-36

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)

## Numbers 15:34 (id: 22683)

Although it was clear that the man was guilty, the community might have been unsure about his punishment. The death sentence was appropriate because ignoring the Sabbath showed a rejection of God's gracious covenant with Israel ([Exodus 31:12–17](https://ref.ly/Exod31:12-Exod31:17)). It was similar to blasphemy ([Numbers 15:30](https://ref.ly/Num15:30); [Leviticus 24:10–16](https://ref.ly/Lev24:10-Lev24:16); compare [1 Timothy 1:20](https://ref.ly/1Tim1:20)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 15:34


## Numbers 15:37–41 (id: 22684)

* If the Hebrews had remembered their special relationship with the Lord, they might have avoided the problems in Numbers. The *tassels* reminded Israel of their unique bond with a holy God and his expectations. He had freed his people from Egypt. Consistent with his call to a covenant relationship, he expected them to observe the law (see [Deuteronomy 6:6–9](https://ref.ly/Deut6:6-Deut6:9); [Matthew 9:20](https://ref.ly/Matt9:20); [14:36](https://ref.ly/Matt14:36); [23:5](https://ref.ly/Matt23:5)).
* The color *blue* was a sign of royalty, which means kingship or rule. It also reminded the people of God’s holiness.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 15:37-41

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Tassel (ID: `realia:Tassel`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Play the Prostitute (ID: `keyterm:PlayTheProstitute`); Purple Cloth (ID: `realia:PurpleCloth`); Thread (ID: `realia:Thread`); Measuring Reed (ID: `realia:MeasuringReed`)

## Numbers 16:1–17.13 (id: 22686)

Another rebellion demanded a clear separation of duties between priests and Levites. This power struggle was more serious than previous rebellions against God's authority (chapter [11](https://ref.ly/Num11:1-Num11:35); [12:1–15](https://ref.ly/Num12:1-Num12:15); [14:1–45](https://ref.ly/Num14:1-Num14:45)). 

In these historical events, the rebels protested minor issues and questioned their leaders' actions. Yet, Moses reminded them that they were actually rebelling against God. As a result, the Israelites suffered deadly consequences for their rebellion.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 16:1-17:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Korah (ID: `person:Korah.3`); Abiram (ID: `person:Abiram`); Levi (ID: `person:Levi.3`); Dathan (ID: `person:Dathan`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Tribe of Levi (ID: `group:Levi`); Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`); Rod (ID: `realia:Rod`); Shovel (ID: `realia:Shovel`); Testimony (ID: `keyterm:Witness.2`); Plague (ID: `keyterm:Blow`); Eliab (ID: `person:Eliab.2`); Hell (ID: `place:Hell`); Eleazar (ID: `person:Eleazar.2`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Appease (ID: `keyterm:Appease`); Land Flowing of Milk and Honey (ID: `keyterm:LandFlowingOfMilkAndHoney`); Temple Altar (ID: `realia:TempleAltar`); Stone Altar (ID: `realia:StoneAltar`); Tabernacle Altar (ID: `realia:TabernacleAltar`); Altar (ID: `realia:Altar`); On (ID: `person:On`); Peleth (ID: `person:Peleth`); Reuben (ID: `person:Reuben`); Kohath (ID: `person:Kohath`)

## Numbers 16:3 (id: 22701)

The rebels "envied Moses, as well as Aaron" ([Psalm 106:16](https://ref.ly/Ps106:16)). They claimed Moses and Aaron had overstepped their authority because all Israelites were God's chosen people ([Exodus 19:6](https://ref.ly/Exod19:6)). Their protest for equality against Moses and Aaron's authority probably intended to replace it with their own.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 16:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`)

## Numbers 16:4–7 (id: 22702)

*Moses … fell facedown* on the ground to pray (compare [14:5](https://ref.ly/Num14:5); [16:22](https://ref.ly/Num16:22)). Then he challenged Korah and his followers to a test.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 16:4-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Korah (ID: `person:Korah.3`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Levi (ID: `person:Levi.3`); Tribe of Levi (ID: `group:Levi`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Shovel (ID: `realia:Shovel`)

## Numbers 16:6 (id: 22706)

*Take censers:* Korah and his group were to act like priests by burning incense (as in [Leviticus 16:11–13](https://ref.ly/Lev16:11-Lev16:13)). By doing this, they were claiming the right to serve as priests. God himself would decide who truly had the right to serve in that role.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 16:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Korah (ID: `person:Korah.3`); Shovel (ID: `realia:Shovel`)

## Numbers 16:10 (id: 22687)

Chapters [3–4](https://ref.ly/Num3:1-Num4:49) describe the special ministry duties of the Levites. Aaron's descendants had the priesthood ([3:1–4](https://ref.ly/Num3:1-Num3:4)). The rebels complained against the Lord, not Moses or Aaron.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 16:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Levi (ID: `person:Levi.3`); Priesthood (ID: `keyterm:Priesthood`); Tribe of Levi (ID: `group:Levi`)

## Numbers 16:13 (id: 22688)

*land flowing with milk and honey:* This phrase repeats the words the spies used earlier to describe Canaan ([Numbers 13:27](https://ref.ly/Num13:27)). In this story, however, the words are used with sarcasm.

The speakers twist the meaning on purpose. They use this phrase to describe Egypt in a positive way. This distorts the truth about life in Egypt, where the Israelites had lived as slaves (see study note on 11:4–15).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 16:13
* **Associated Articles:** Numbers 11:4-15 (ID: `22629@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Land Flowing of Milk and Honey (ID: `keyterm:LandFlowingOfMilkAndHoney`)

## Numbers 16:14 (id: 22689)

* The Israelites were leaving their new homeland because they had rebelled against Moses. He had done everything possible to bring them to that land (chapters [13–14](https://ref.ly/Num13:1-Num14:45)).
* *Will you gouge out the eyes of these men?:* They accused Moses and Aaron of trying to mislead the Israelites.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 16:14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Cause to Possess (ID: `keyterm:Possess`); Land Flowing of Milk and Honey (ID: `keyterm:LandFlowingOfMilkAndHoney`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`)

*Do not regard their offering:* Compare [Psalm 109:7](https://ref.ly/Ps109:7) and [Proverbs 28:9](https://ref.ly/Prov28:9).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 16:15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Donkey (ID: `fauna:Donkey`)

Moses gave final instructions for a test to see if the Lord would accept the rebels' offering or Aaron's offering.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 16:16-17

## Numbers 16:18–22 (id: 22692)

At the height of the rebellion, the Lord intervened to save Moses and Aaron*.* Moses and Aaron then prayed for the people.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 16:18-22
