## Numbers 16:19 (id: 22693)

*had gathered his whole assembly … at the entrance of the Tent of Meeting:* This was either to watch the test or to remove Moses and Aaron from leadership.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 16:19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Korah (ID: `person:Korah.3`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`)

## Numbers 16:21–26 (id: 22694)

The Lord threatened to destroy the entire community because of widespread sin (compare [Genesis 18:16–33](https://ref.ly/Gen18:16-Gen18:33)). Moses and Aaron prayed to limit the Lord's judgment to those guilty of this specific rebellion.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 16:21-26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abiram (ID: `person:Abiram`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Dathan (ID: `person:Dathan`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Korah (ID: `person:Korah.3`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`)

## Numbers 16:26 (id: 22695)

*Do not touch anything that belongs to them:* These men and their belongings were now "set apart for destruction" (see [Leviticus 27:28–29](https://ref.ly/Lev27:28-Lev27:29); compare [Deuteronomy 7:26](https://ref.ly/Deut7:26); [13:17](https://ref.ly/Deut13:17); [Joshua 6:18](https://ref.ly/Josh6:18); [7:1–26](https://ref.ly/Josh7:1-Josh7:26)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 16:26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`)

## Numbers 16:28–30 (id: 22696)

* Moses believed that God would deliver justice, and God did so ([16:31–35](https://ref.ly/Num16:31-Num16:35); compare [1 Kings 18:18–40](https://ref.ly/1Kgs18:18-1Kgs18:40)).
* *into Sheol:* This was the place of the dead in the Old Testament. Sheol describes the realm where people go after death (see [1 Samuel 2:6](https://ref.ly/1Sam2:6); [2 Samuel 22:5–6](https://ref.ly/2Sam22:5-2Sam22:6); [Job 3:20–22](https://ref.ly/Job3:20-Job3:22); [Psalm 16:10](https://ref.ly/Ps16:10); [Acts 2:27–33](https://ref.ly/Acts2:27-Acts2:33); [1 Corinthians 15:15](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:15); [Revelation 20:13–14](https://ref.ly/Rev20:13-Rev20:14)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 16:28-30

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Visitation (ID: `keyterm:Visitation`); Hell (ID: `place:Hell`)

## Numbers 16:31–33 (id: 22697)

Korah’s sons did not die at this time ([26:10–11](https://ref.ly/Num26:10-Num26:11)). Later, his descendants became gatekeepers and musicians in the temple ([1 Chronicles 9:19](https://ref.ly/1Chr9:19), [31](https://ref.ly/1Chr9:31); [26:19](https://ref.ly/1Chr26:19); [2 Chronicles 20:19](https://ref.ly/2Chr20:19); see the titles of [Psalms 42](https://ref.ly/Ps42:1-Ps42:11); [45–49](https://ref.ly/Ps45:1-Ps49:20); [84–85](https://ref.ly/Ps84:1-Ps85:13); [87–88](https://ref.ly/Ps87:1-Ps88:18)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 16:31-33

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Korah (ID: `person:Korah.3`); Hell (ID: `place:Hell`)

## Numbers 16:34–35 (id: 22698)

The 250 Israelite leaders tried to offer incense even though they were not priests. Their fate was like that of Nadab and Abihu, who also offered unauthorized fire before the Lord ([Leviticus 10:1–2](https://ref.ly/Lev10:1-Lev10:2)). They also shared the same judgment as those who complained about the hardships in the wilderness ([Numbers 11:1](https://ref.ly/Num11:1); compare [2 Kings 1:10](https://ref.ly/2Kgs1:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 16:34-35

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Numbers 16:37 (id: 22699)

Although the men who used the incense burners were wicked, the vessels themselves were holy and had to be treated with care and respect. The burning coals were also holy and had to be scattered so that they would not be used for any other purpose.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 16:37

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Eleazar (ID: `person:Eleazar.2`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Shovel (ID: `realia:Shovel`)

## Numbers 16:38–40 (id: 22700)

Eleazar made a bronze sheet to cover the altar, replacing or adding to the earlier one ([Exodus 38:2](https://ref.ly/Exod38:2)). Like the plain blue tassels in [Numbers 15:37–41](https://ref.ly/Num15:37-Num15:41), it reminded the people of Israel to obey God.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 16:38-40

The Israelites continued their self\-destructive and defiant behavior, which caused their problems. They then blamed Moses and Aaron for the events.

Moses and Aaron quickly acted to save the Israelites from God's anger. Aaron used an incense burner with hot coals among the people to stop the plague.

## Numbers 17:1–5 (id: 22708)

The shepherd's *staff* was a common item (see [Psalm 23:4](https://ref.ly/Ps23:4)). Like the scepter, it symbolized authority (see [Jeremiah 48:17](https://ref.ly/Jer48:17)). This trial was fitting because the Hebrew words for "staffs" and "tribes" sound the same. The Lord's chosen staff would show his chosen tribe.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 17:1-5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Rod (ID: `realia:Rod`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Testimony (ID: `keyterm:Witness.2`); Levi (ID: `person:Levi.3`); Tribe of Levi (ID: `group:Levi`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`)

## Numbers 17:1–13 (id: 22707)

The Lord gave another test to make the lesson clear. This test repeated the teaching from the events in chapter [16](https://ref.ly/Num16:1-Num16:50). It also prepared the people for the instructions that follow in chapter [18](https://ref.ly/Num18:1-Num18:32).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 17:1-13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Rod (ID: `realia:Rod`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Testimony (ID: `keyterm:Witness.2`); Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`); Levi (ID: `person:Levi.3`); Tribe of Levi (ID: `group:Levi`); Complete (ID: `keyterm:Complete`); Almond (ID: `flora:Almond`)

## Numbers 17:4 (id: 22710)

The staffs were in the Lord's presence, a safe place where no one could interfere with them ([17:7](https://ref.ly/Num17:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 17:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Testimony (ID: `keyterm:Witness.2`); Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`)

## Numbers 17:8 (id: 22711)

* *Aaron’s staff … produced almonds:* This proved he was God's chosen leader and that the Levites had a special role. The cups on the lampstand of the Tent of the Testimony (the tabernacle) had the shape of almond blossoms ([Exodus 25:33–36](https://ref.ly/Exod25:33-Exod25:36)). Later, the prophet Jeremiah’s vision of an almond branch symbolized the Lord's watchfulness and his plans to act ([Jeremiah 1:11–12](https://ref.ly/Jer1:11-Jer1:12)).
* There is no natural reason for Aaron’s staff to bud. Almond wood is soft when alive and only hardens enough for use as a staff after it has dried.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 17:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Testimony (ID: `keyterm:Witness.2`); Levi (ID: `person:Levi.3`); Tribe of Levi (ID: `group:Levi`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`); Rod (ID: `realia:Rod`); Almond (ID: `flora:Almond`)

## Numbers 17:9–11 (id: 22712)

After each tribal leader claimed his staff, it was clear that Aaron’s deserved special treatment. They placed it permanently in front of the ark of the covenant to warn potential rebels. It would also warn Israelites who complained about the Lord and his appointed leaders. The ark of the covenant eventually contained this staff, a jar of manna, and the stone tablets of the law (compare [Hebrews 9:4](https://ref.ly/Heb9:4)). This was to remind the Israelites of their time wandering in the wilderness.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 17:9-11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Rod (ID: `realia:Rod`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Testimony (ID: `keyterm:Witness.2`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)

## Numbers 17:12–13 (id: 22709)

The Lord and his tabernacle were holy. Because of God's holiness, his holy place could be dangerous for unqualified people. Moses worked hard to help the people understand and respect the God of Israel. His efforts were becoming successful.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 17:12-13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Complete (ID: `keyterm:Complete`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Numbers 18:1–4 (id: 22714)

The Lord gave special authority to Aaron, his sons (the priests), and his relatives (the Levites). They were responsible to God for offenses against the holy place. Yet, only the priests were responsible to God for their own violations of God's instructions for their service.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 18:1-4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`); Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`); Tribe of Levi (ID: `group:Levi`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Testimony (ID: `keyterm:Witness.2`); Levi (ID: `person:Levi.3`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); Bear the Consequences Of (ID: `keyterm:BearTheConsequencesOf`); Priesthood (ID: `keyterm:Priesthood`); Stone Altar (ID: `realia:StoneAltar`); Tabernacle Altar (ID: `realia:TabernacleAltar`); Temple Altar (ID: `realia:TempleAltar`); Altar (ID: `realia:Altar`)

## Numbers 18:1–32 (id: 22713)

The instructions here came from the need shown in chapter [17](https://ref.ly/Num17:1-Num17:13) for clear boundaries between the people, priests, and Levites. The Levites had special privileges and important responsibilities. One key part of their job was to protect the Tent of Meeting from unauthorized entry (chapter [16](https://ref.ly/Num16:1-Num16:50)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 18:1-32

## Numbers 18:3–4 (id: 22721)

* *all the duties:* See [1:47–53](https://ref.ly/Num1:47-Num1:53); [3:5–38](https://ref.ly/Num3:5-Num3:38); [4:1–33](https://ref.ly/Num4:1-Num4:33).
* *they must not come near**:* The Levites could approach only after they had covered the holy objects for transport.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 18:3-4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`); Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`); Stone Altar (ID: `realia:StoneAltar`); Tabernacle Altar (ID: `realia:TabernacleAltar`); Temple Altar (ID: `realia:TempleAltar`); Altar (ID: `realia:Altar`)

## Numbers 18:5 (id: 22725)

Aaron and the priests represented the people in the presence of the Lord. Their actions affected all of Israel (see [16:18–21](https://ref.ly/Num16:18-Num16:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 18:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Stone Altar (ID: `realia:StoneAltar`); Tabernacle Altar (ID: `realia:TabernacleAltar`); Temple Altar (ID: `realia:TempleAltar`); Altar (ID: `realia:Altar`)

## Numbers 18:5–7 (id: 22724)

Only the priests, who were Aaron and his descendants, could perform holy duties inside the sanctuary or approach the altar. This altar was a holy area that deserved special honor. Only the high priest could serve in the Most Holy Place. The Levites could only serve in the courtyard, away from the altar. The exception was when it was time to assemble or disassemble the Tent of Meeting (chapter [4](https://ref.ly/Num4:1-Num4:49)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 18:5-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Force to Serve (ID: `keyterm:EnticedToServe`); Stone Altar (ID: `realia:StoneAltar`); Tabernacle Altar (ID: `realia:TabernacleAltar`); Temple Altar (ID: `realia:TempleAltar`); Altar (ID: `realia:Altar`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Levi (ID: `person:Levi.3`); Tribe of Levi (ID: `group:Levi`); Priesthood (ID: `keyterm:Priesthood`); Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`); Curtain (ID: `realia:Curtain`)

## Numbers 18:6–7 (id: 22726)

*Levites … as a gift to you:* [Numbers 3–4](https://ref.ly/Num3:1-Num4:49) explains this phrase. See also [3:9](https://ref.ly/Num3:9); [8:16](https://ref.ly/Num8:16), [19](https://ref.ly/Num8:19).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 18:6-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Force to Serve (ID: `keyterm:EnticedToServe`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Levi (ID: `person:Levi.3`); Tribe of Levi (ID: `group:Levi`); Priesthood (ID: `keyterm:Priesthood`); Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`); Curtain (ID: `realia:Curtain`); Stone Altar (ID: `realia:StoneAltar`); Tabernacle Altar (ID: `realia:TabernacleAltar`); Temple Altar (ID: `realia:TempleAltar`); Altar (ID: `realia:Altar`)

## Numbers 18:7 (id: 22727)

*any outsider … must be put to death:*This verse warned all Israelites to avoid areas where God did not allow them to be. God prohibited access to certain holy places for those not qualified in a religious sense. Only the holiest members of the community could access these holy areas. Ignoring this rule meant risking one's life because it violated God's holiness (see also [1:51](https://ref.ly/Num1:51), [53](https://ref.ly/Num1:53); [3:10](https://ref.ly/Num3:10), [29–38](https://ref.ly/Num3:29-Num3:38); [4:1–20](https://ref.ly/Num4:1-Num4:20)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 18:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Force to Serve (ID: `keyterm:EnticedToServe`); Priesthood (ID: `keyterm:Priesthood`); Curtain (ID: `realia:Curtain`); Tabernacle Altar (ID: `realia:TabernacleAltar`); Stone Altar (ID: `realia:StoneAltar`); Temple Altar (ID: `realia:TempleAltar`); Altar (ID: `realia:Altar`)

## Numbers 18:8–14 (id: 22728)

The priests and Levites received support through the sacrificial system (see, for example, [Leviticus 7:28–36](https://ref.ly/Lev7:28-Lev7:36)). God's chosen representatives deserve payment for their work (compare [Matthew 10:9–10](https://ref.ly/Matt10:9-Matt10:10); [1 Corinthians 9:3–10](https://ref.ly/1Cor9:3-1Cor9:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 18:8-14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Pure (ID: `keyterm:Pure`); Decree (ID: `keyterm:Decree`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Something Devoted to God (ID: `keyterm:SomethingDevotedToGod`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Must (ID: `realia:Must`); Grain (ID: `flora:Grain`); Anointing Oil (ID: `realia:AnointingOil`); Olive Tree (ID: `flora:OliveTree`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`); Mule (ID: `fauna:Mule`); Olive Oil (ID: `realia:OliveOil`)

## Numbers 18:9–11 (id: 22729)

* Only Aaron and his sons, the priests set apart to serve in God's presence, consumed the most holy offerings ([Exodus 28:1](https://ref.ly/Exod28:1); [29:44–46](https://ref.ly/Exod29:44-Exod29:46)).
* This portion was a holy offering given to God, and the priests had to treat it with respect ([Numbers 18:10](https://ref.ly/Num18:10)). Other types of offerings provided food for the priests and their families, but only if they were ceremonially clean ([18:11–12](https://ref.ly/Num18:11-Num18:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 18:9-11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Pure (ID: `keyterm:Pure`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Decree (ID: `keyterm:Decree`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`)

## Numbers 18:14 (id: 22715)

Offerings set apart for the Lord were available to Aaron and his extended family. The Hebrew term here means a thing or person totally set apart to the Lord. This could involve either destroying or giving the person of thing as an offering. This verse speaks of an offering (see [Leviticus 27:28–29](https://ref.ly/Lev27:28-Lev27:29)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 18:14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Something Devoted to God (ID: `keyterm:SomethingDevotedToGod`)

## Numbers 18:15–16 (id: 22716)

The Lord supported the Hebrew priests and their families from the offerings set aside for him. The priests received firstborn animal offerings and the redemption fee for Israel’s firstborn sons. People had to redeem both firstborn sons and the offspring of unclean animals with a silver fee, since they could not sacrifice either one (see [3:13](https://ref.ly/Num3:13), [40–51](https://ref.ly/Num3:40-Num3:51); [Exodus 13:2](https://ref.ly/Exod13:2)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 18:15-16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Redeem (ID: `keyterm:Redeem`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Defilement (ID: `keyterm:Defilement`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Shekel (ID: `realia:Shekel`); Gerah (ID: `realia:Gerah`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`)

## Numbers 18:19 (id: 22717)

*a covenant of salt:* These arrangements were part of an eternal and unbreakable covenant (see [2 Chronicles 13:5](https://ref.ly/2Chr13:5)). The agreement was meant to be permanent because salt, a preservative, symbolized permanence ([Leviticus 2:13](https://ref.ly/Lev2:13)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 18:19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Decree (ID: `keyterm:Decree`)

## Numbers 18:20–21 (id: 22718)

* The priests and Levites did not have a tribal homeland. They lived scattered among Israel's other tribes (see [35:1–8](https://ref.ly/Num35:1-Num35:8); [Deuteronomy 12:12](https://ref.ly/Deut12:12); [Joshua 21:1–42](https://ref.ly/Josh21:1-Josh21:42)).
* *I* *am your portion and your inheritance**:* Instead of land, Israel's priests had assurance that God himself would be their homeland. The tribe of Levi received tithes as payment for their holy service.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 18:20-21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Cause to Possess (ID: `keyterm:Possess`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Tribe of Levi (ID: `group:Levi`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Levi (ID: `person:Levi.3`); Force to Serve (ID: `keyterm:EnticedToServe`); Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`)

## Numbers 18:23–24 (id: 22719)

The Israelites gave a tithe, which was one\-tenth of their yearly income (see [Deuteronomy 14:22–29](https://ref.ly/Deut14:22-Deut14:29); [26:12](https://ref.ly/Deut26:12); [Nehemiah 10:35–39](https://ref.ly/Neh10:35-Neh10:39); [Malachi 3:8–12](https://ref.ly/Mal3:8-Mal3:12); [Matthew 23:23](https://ref.ly/Matt23:23); compare [Genesis 14:17–20](https://ref.ly/Gen14:17-Gen14:20); [Hebrews 7:5–10](https://ref.ly/Heb7:5-Heb7:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 18:23-24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Cause to Possess (ID: `keyterm:Possess`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Levi (ID: `person:Levi.3`); Tribe of Levi (ID: `group:Levi`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); Bear the Consequences Of (ID: `keyterm:BearTheConsequencesOf`); Force to Serve (ID: `keyterm:EnticedToServe`); Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`)

## Numbers 18:25–32 (id: 22720)

The Levites received a tithe (one\-tenth) of the goods of the Israelites ([18:24](https://ref.ly/Num18:24)). However, they were not allowed to keep all of it. They also had to give a tithe of that tithe, which means one\-tenth of the one\-tenth. They gave this portion to the priests as their own harvest offering ([18:27](https://ref.ly/Num18:27)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 18:25-32

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Levi (ID: `person:Levi.3`); Tribe of Levi (ID: `group:Levi`); Threshing Floor (ID: `realia:ThreshingFloor`); Winepress (ID: `realia:Winepress`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); To Cause to Possess (ID: `keyterm:Possess`); To Dishonor (ID: `keyterm:Dishonor`); Bear the Consequences Of (ID: `keyterm:BearTheConsequencesOf`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`); Grain (ID: `flora:Grain`)

## Numbers 18:31 (id: 22722)

Levites and their families could eat any food received as tithes. This food was their payment for working at the Tent of Meeting.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 18:31

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`)

## Numbers 18:32 (id: 22723)

The food tithes were holy gifts from the people of Israel. They could not use these gifts for something common by failing to give a portion to the priests.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 18:32

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); To Dishonor (ID: `keyterm:Dishonor`); Bear the Consequences Of (ID: `keyterm:BearTheConsequencesOf`)

## Numbers 19:1–10 (id: 22730)

They made the water for the purification ceremony with ashes of the red heifer ([19:9](https://ref.ly/Num19:9)). It might be the same as the water of purification in [8:7](https://ref.ly/Num8:7) (see also [31:19–24](https://ref.ly/Num31:19-Num31:24)). This ceremony needed the ashes of a red heifer. The color probably represented blood (compare [Hebrews 9:13–14](https://ref.ly/Heb9:13-Heb9:14)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 19:1-10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Defilement (ID: `keyterm:Defilement`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Eleazar (ID: `person:Eleazar.2`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Pure (ID: `keyterm:Pure`); Complete (ID: `keyterm:Complete`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); To Cleanse (ID: `keyterm:Cleanse.2`); Instruction (ID: `keyterm:Instruction`); Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`); Cedar (ID: `flora:Cedar`); Purple Cloth (ID: `realia:PurpleCloth`); Hyssop (ID: `flora:Hyssop`); Yoke (ID: `realia:Yoke`)

## Numbers 19:1–22 (id: 22731)

This section explains how ordinary Israelites can become ceremonially clean again after touching a corpse and avoid defiling the Tent of Meeting (see [9:4–12](https://ref.ly/Num9:4-Num9:12); [Leviticus 17:15](https://ref.ly/Lev17:15); [21:1–4](https://ref.ly/Lev21:1-Lev21:4), [11–12](https://ref.ly/Lev21:11-Lev21:12)). God's holiness demands that the place of his presence remains pure and separate from pollution (compare [1 Corinthians 6:9–11](https://ref.ly/1Cor6:9-1Cor6:11), [18–20](https://ref.ly/1Cor6:18-1Cor6:20)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 19:1-22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Defilement (ID: `keyterm:Defilement`); To Cleanse (ID: `keyterm:Cleanse.2`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Pure (ID: `keyterm:Pure`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Eleazar (ID: `person:Eleazar.2`); Instruction (ID: `keyterm:Instruction`); Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`); Hyssop (ID: `flora:Hyssop`); Complete (ID: `keyterm:Complete`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Cedar (ID: `flora:Cedar`); Thread (ID: `realia:Thread`); Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`); Measuring Reed (ID: `realia:MeasuringReed`); Yoke (ID: `realia:Yoke`); Purple Cloth (ID: `realia:PurpleCloth`)

## Numbers 19:3–5 (id: 22736)

To protect Aaron’s holy status, Eleazar had to perform the red heifer offering because it caused ceremonial impurity (see [Leviticus 21:10–12](https://ref.ly/Lev21:10-Lev21:12)). The red heifer ceremony differed from the regular sacrifice (compare [Leviticus 4:3–12](https://ref.ly/Lev4:3-Lev4:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 19:3-5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Eleazar (ID: `person:Eleazar.2`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`)

## Numbers 19:6 (id: 22737)

The only other passage where cedar wood, hyssop, and scarlet wool appear together is [Leviticus 14](https://ref.ly/Lev14:1-Lev14:57). That chapter describes another ceremony (also "outside the camp") that resulted in ceremonial purification.

Some scholars suggest Eleazar added cedar wood and hyssop for their aroma. Hyssop is associated with purification ([Exodus 12:22](https://ref.ly/Exod12:22); [Psalm 51:7](https://ref.ly/Ps51:7); compare [Hebrews 9:19](https://ref.ly/Heb9:19)). The scarlet yarn, like the red heifer, probably symbolizes blood. Together, the cedar wood, hyssop, and scarlet wool symbolize cleansing.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 19:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Purple Cloth (ID: `realia:PurpleCloth`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Hyssop (ID: `flora:Hyssop`); Cedar (ID: `flora:Cedar`)

## Numbers 19:7–10 (id: 22738)

It required three people to produce and collect the heifer’s ashes. These were Eleazar, the person who burned the animal, and the person who gathered its ashes. All three became ceremonially impure for a day because of this process. The same was true for the person who used the finished product. This was a potion made from the ash dust and water (compare [19:21](https://ref.ly/Num19:21)). This potion had the same defiling effect as blood associated with sacrifices ([Leviticus 6:27–28](https://ref.ly/Lev6:27-Lev6:28)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 19:7-10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Defilement (ID: `keyterm:Defilement`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Pure (ID: `keyterm:Pure`); To Cleanse (ID: `keyterm:Cleanse.2`)

## Numbers 19:9 (id: 22739)

The ceremony of burning the red heifer intended to produce ashes. They collected these ashes and stored them in a pure place outside the camp. The Israelites used them in water for purification ceremonies to remove pollution, such as contact with a corpse ([19:11–22](https://ref.ly/Num19:11-Num19:22)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 19:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Pure (ID: `keyterm:Pure`); To Cleanse (ID: `keyterm:Cleanse.2`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`)

## Numbers 19:11–19 (id: 22732)

If someone touched a human dead body, that person became ceremonially unclean for seven days (compare [Leviticus 21:1–3](https://ref.ly/Lev21:1-Lev21:3)). This meant a person was not allowed to take part in worship until they were made clean. 

To become clean again, the person had to use the water of purification. This water was sprinkled on the third day and again on the seventh day.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 19:11-19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Defilement (ID: `keyterm:Defilement`); Pure (ID: `keyterm:Pure`); To Cleanse (ID: `keyterm:Cleanse.2`); Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Instruction (ID: `keyterm:Instruction`); Thread (ID: `realia:Thread`); Hyssop (ID: `flora:Hyssop`); Measuring Reed (ID: `realia:MeasuringReed`)

## Numbers 19:13 (id: 22733)

Becoming unclean by touching a dead body was very serious. A person who was ceremonially defiled (not fit for worship) could also make the Lord’s tabernacle unclean (compare [Leviticus 15:31](https://ref.ly/Lev15:31)). 

Because of this danger, such impurity could lead to being “cut off” from the community of Israel. This meant being removed from the people, and in some cases, it could mean death (see [Numbers 9:13](https://ref.ly/Num9:13); [Exodus 12:15](https://ref.ly/Exod12:15); [Leviticus 7:20–21](https://ref.ly/Lev7:20-Lev7:21); [Leviticus 17:4–14](https://ref.ly/Lev17:4-Lev17:14); [Leviticus 20:17–18](https://ref.ly/Lev20:17-Lev20:18)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 19:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Defilement (ID: `keyterm:Defilement`); To Cleanse (ID: `keyterm:Cleanse.2`)

## Numbers 19:14–16 (id: 22734)

Death made a person ceremonially unclean, and this uncleanness could spread. It could pass to an object or to a place where someone had died (compare [Leviticus 15:1–12](https://ref.ly/Lev15:1-Lev15:12)). This means that anything connected to the dead body could also become unclean. People had to be careful so that impurity did not spread through the camp.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 19:14-16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Defilement (ID: `keyterm:Defilement`); Instruction (ID: `keyterm:Instruction`); Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`); Thread (ID: `realia:Thread`); Measuring Reed (ID: `realia:MeasuringReed`)

## Numbers 19:17–22 (id: 22735)

To cleanse with the heifer's ashes, they made a special purifying mixture. They combined ashes from the burnt purification offering with fresh water (compare [5:17](https://ref.ly/Num5:17)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 19:17-22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Defilement (ID: `keyterm:Defilement`); Pure (ID: `keyterm:Pure`); To Cleanse (ID: `keyterm:Cleanse.2`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`); Hyssop (ID: `flora:Hyssop`)

## Numbers 20:1 (id: 22753)

Everyone in Miriam's generation also died ([14:26–30](https://ref.ly/Num14:26-Num14:30)). This included Moses and Aaron ([20:2–13](https://ref.ly/Num20:2-Num20:13), [22–29](https://ref.ly/Num20:22-Num20:29)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 20:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Miriam (ID: `person:Miriam`); Wilderness of Zin (ID: `place:Zin`); Kadesh-barnea (ID: `place:Kadesh-barnea`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)

## Numbers 20:2 (id: 22758)

*there was no water:* Most of the year, Sinai and the Negev are very hot and dry.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 20:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`)

## Numbers 20:2–13 (id: 22754)

The people of Israel complained again about not having water and food (compare [11:4–35](https://ref.ly/Num11:4-Num11:35); [21:4–5](https://ref.ly/Num21:4-Num21:5); [Exodus 15:22–25](https://ref.ly/Exod15:22-Exod15:25)). So, Moses and Aaron disobeyed the Lord. As a result, they lost the privilege of entering the promised land.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 20:2-13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Rod (ID: `realia:Rod`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Meribah (ID: `place:Meribah.2`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Believe In (ID: `keyterm:BelieveIn`); Rebel (ID: `keyterm:Rebel`); Pomegranate (ID: `flora:Pomegranate`); Coriander (ID: `flora:Coriander`); Fig (ID: `flora:Fig`); Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`); Barley (ID: `flora:Barley`)

## Numbers 20:3 (id: 22759)

*our brothers:* This shows that the Israelites saw themselves as connected to those who had died:

* Nadab and Abihu ([Leviticus 10:1–5](https://ref.ly/Lev10:1-Lev10:5)),
* those who died during the first plague after craving meat ([Numbers 11:33](https://ref.ly/Num11:33)),
* the skeptics who died in the wilderness ([14:29](https://ref.ly/Num14:29)),
* the ten unfaithful spies ([14:36–37](https://ref.ly/Num14:36-Num14:37)),
* Korah, Abiram, and Dathan ([16:32](https://ref.ly/Num16:32)),
* the 250 leaders ([16:35](https://ref.ly/Num16:35)), and
* the 14,700 people who died in the second plague ([16:49](https://ref.ly/Num16:49)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 20:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`)

## Numbers 20:6 (id: 22760)

*They fell facedown:* As in earlier conflicts, they lay down and waited for the Lord's glorious presence to resolve the crisis ([14:5](https://ref.ly/Num14:5), [10](https://ref.ly/Num14:10); [16:4](https://ref.ly/Num16:4), [19](https://ref.ly/Num16:19), [22](https://ref.ly/Num16:22)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 20:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`)

## Numbers 20:7–9 (id: 22761)

God heard Israel’s complaints and accepted them as valid. He did not ignore their concerns.

Then God told Moses to deal with the problem and provide a solution.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 20:7-9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Rod (ID: `realia:Rod`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`)

## Numbers 20:8–9 (id: 22762)

Moses placed the staff in front of the ark of the covenant to stop the Israelites from complaining (compare [17:10](https://ref.ly/Num17:10)). The staff represented God's authority given to Moses and Aaron. God's instructions to his representatives are very precise and require exact obedience (compare [James 3:1](https://ref.ly/Jas3:1); also see [2 Timothy 2:24–26](https://ref.ly/2Tim2:24-2Tim2:26)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 20:8-9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Rod (ID: `realia:Rod`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`)

## Numbers 20:10–12 (id: 22747)

Instead of obeying God's instructions with respect, Moses criticized the people (*Must we bring you water out of this rock?*). Moses then hit the rock twice with his staff ([20:11](https://ref.ly/Num20:11)). God considered the words and actions of Moses as a serious lack of faith. Moses "spoke rashly" ([Psalm 106:32–33](https://ref.ly/Ps106:32-Ps106:33); compare [James 1:20](https://ref.ly/Jas1:20)). Moses did not trust God enough to show God’s holiness to the people.

God had told Moses exactly what to do. By acting differently, Moses showed a lack of faith that God would provide for his people. His angry words also revealed this lack of trust.

The Lord requires obedience and faith. Anything less dishonors his holy character ([Numbers 20:24](https://ref.ly/Num20:24); [27:14](https://ref.ly/Num27:14)). Such a serious lack of obedience and faith led to the same outcome as earlier ([14:20–23](https://ref.ly/Num14:20-Num14:23); see also [1 Corinthians 10:4–12](https://ref.ly/1Cor10:4-1Cor10:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 20:10-12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Believe In (ID: `keyterm:BelieveIn`); Rebel (ID: `keyterm:Rebel`); Rod (ID: `realia:Rod`)

## Numbers 20:13 (id: 22748)

* *Meribah* means “Arguing” or “Quarreling.” As they had earlier at Rephidim([Exodus 17:1–7](https://ref.ly/Exod17:1-Exod17:7)), the Israelites gave a name that described what happened there. The name pointed more to the event than to the place itself.
* At Meribah, God showed his holiness through judgment. He disciplined both Aaron and Moses for their sin.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 20:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Meribah (ID: `place:Meribah.2`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`)

## Numbers 20:14–21 (id: 22750)

Israel tried to make peace with Edom, but they did not succeed. The two nations had not had contact since the time when Esau (also called Edom) and Jacob (also called Israel) buried their father Isaac([Genesis 35:29](https://ref.ly/Gen35:29); [36:6–8](https://ref.ly/Gen36:6-Gen36:8); see also [Genesis 32:28](https://ref.ly/Gen32:28)). 

Moses sent a formal message to the king of Edom. His request followed the style of official letters used in that time. While Israel often fought with the Canaanites and other groups, they spoke politely to Edom because the two peoples were related (see also [Deuteronomy 2:4](https://ref.ly/Deut2:4); [23:7](https://ref.ly/Deut23:7)). 

Even so, family ties were not strong enough to remove the old tension between Jacob and Esau ([Genesis 27:41](https://ref.ly/Gen27:41)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 20:14-21

## Numbers 20:14–21.35 (id: 22749)

The Israelites started their journey to the promised land here. They moved from Kadesh, leaving the Sinai peninsula. They entered the area east of the Jordan River, known as Transjordan. They approached the promised land from the east, taking a less direct route (see [14:25](https://ref.ly/Num14:25); compare [Deuteronomy 2:2–6](https://ref.ly/Deut2:2-Deut2:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 20:14-21

## Numbers 20:17 (id: 22751)

* *We will not …* *drink water from any well**:* Edom was a very dry land. Food and water were limited, and there was not enough to support such a large group of Hebrew travelers.
* *We will stay on the King’s Highway**:* This road is mentioned several times in the Old Testament, but its exact path is not fully known (also [21:22](https://ref.ly/Num21:22)). It was an official road used for government business, trade, and the movement of armies.

    Along this road, travelers could find towns, food, and water between the Red Sea and Damascus. The road remained important during Roman times and even later. It was different from another route farther east called the Desert Highway, which was much harder and less welcoming for travelers.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 20:17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** King’s Highway (ID: `place:KingsHighway`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`)

## Numbers 20:18–21 (id: 22752)

*You may not travel through our land:* Just as their ancestor Esau did not trust Jacob, the Edomites now did not trust Jacob’s descendants ([Genesis 27:36](https://ref.ly/Gen27:36)). Therefore, Israel had to turn around and find another way to approach Canaan (compare [Deuteronomy 2:4–6](https://ref.ly/Deut2:4-Deut2:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 20:18-21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Edom (ID: `place:Edom`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Messengers (ID: `group:Messengers`)

## Numbers 20:22–23 (id: 22755)

* Israel did not take the shorter route through the southern area east of the Jordan River. That region was the main territory of Edom ([20:21](https://ref.ly/Num20:21)).
* After they left Kadesh, the Hebrews came to Mount Hor. This mountain stood on the border of Edom. The exact location of Mount Hor is not known today. However, the Jewish historian Josephus, who wrote between AD 70 and AD 100, connected it with the region of Petra. He identified it with Jebel Haroun, which means “mountain of Aaron.”

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 20:22-23

Aaron’s participation in the rebellion of his generation resulted in his death outside the promised land ([20:10–12](https://ref.ly/Num20:10-Num20:12)).

## Numbers 20:27–29 (id: 22757)

* Moses transferred priestly authority to Eleazar by giving him Aaron's priestly garments (compare [Leviticus 8:7–9](https://ref.ly/Lev8:7-Lev8:9)). After this, Aaron died at 123 years old, in the fortieth year after the Hebrews left Egypt (see [Numbers 33:37–39](https://ref.ly/Num33:37-Num33:39)).
* *the entire house of Israel mourned for him thirty days:* This was a great honor (see also [Deuteronomy 34:8](https://ref.ly/Deut34:8)). The usual public mourning period was seven days ([Genesis 50:10](https://ref.ly/Gen50:10); [1 Samuel 31:13](https://ref.ly/1Sam31:13)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 20:27-29

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Mount Hor (ID: `place:HorMount`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Eleazar (ID: `person:Eleazar.2`)

## Numbers 21:1 (id: 22770)

* *the Canaanite king of Arad:* It is possible that the Canaanite king of Arad felt threatened by many enemies south of his territory (compare [14:44–45](https://ref.ly/Num14:44-Num14:45)). This may explain why he attacked Israel.
* Arad and Hormah ([21:3](https://ref.ly/Num21:3)) were far north of Mount Hor ([20:22–29](https://ref.ly/Num20:22-Num20:29); [21:4](https://ref.ly/Num21:4); see study note on 20:22–23). Ancient Arad is usually identified with Tell Arad. It is west of the modern Israeli town of Arad and about 80 kilometers (50 miles) north of Kadesh.

    Excavations at Tell Arad have found important remains from the Early Bronze Age (about 3300–2000 BC) and the Iron Age (about 1200–500 BC). However, there is no clear evidence that people lived there during the Late Bronze Age (1500–1200 BC), the time of Israel’s exodus and wilderness journey.

    Scholars have suggested several explanations:

    + Some connect ancient Arad with a different site in the same region.
        + Others argue that these stories were written much later and do not match archaeological discoveries.
        + Still others say the evidence is incomplete and does not allow a final answer.
        Similar questions about geography and archaeology appear in other parts of the Old Testament. In many cases, the available evidence does not give a certain conclusion.

* Atharim is not known from any other source.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 21:1
* **Associated Articles:** Numbers 20:22-23 (ID: `22755@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Atharim (ID: `place:Atharim`); Negev (ID: `place:Negeb`); Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`); Arad (ID: `place:Arad`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Canaanites (ID: `group:Canaan`)

## Numbers 21:1–22.1 (id: 22763)

* As Israel journeyed to the promised land, they traveled from Mount Hor to the plains of Moab ([22:1](https://ref.ly/Num22:1)). The story of Israel's journey emphasizes the Lord's patient plan to lead Israel into Canaan despite their weaknesses.
* The general direction of their travel is clear. However, the exact path they took is not known today. Many of the places mentioned are difficult to identify.

    Archaeological evidence at some sites does not match the time usually connected with the wilderness period. Because of this, there are still serious questions about geography and history in the wilderness journey.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 21:1-22:1

## Numbers 21:2–3 (id: 22771)

* *devote … to destruction*: This phrase translates the Hebrew word *kharam*. This word means to devote something fully to God by destroying it. Israel was told to completely destroy their enemies and their property in certain cases (see [Leviticus 27:28–29](https://ref.ly/Lev27:28-Lev27:29); [Deuteronomy 7:2](https://ref.ly/Deut7:2); [20:17](https://ref.ly/Deut20:17); [Joshua 6–7](https://ref.ly/Josh6:1-Josh7:26); [10:1–11:23](https://ref.ly/Josh10:1-Josh11:23)).
* This battle also includes wordplay between *kharam* and *Hormah* (Hebrew *khormah*, [Numbers 21:3](https://ref.ly/Num21:3)). The similar sounds connect the action of destroying with the name of the place.

    The event marks a turning point in Israel’s dealings with the Canaanites. The Canaanites would continue to be strong and dangerous enemies during the time of the conquest.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 21:2-3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Set Apart for Destruction (ID: `keyterm:Proscribe.2`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`); Vow (ID: `keyterm:Vow`); Canaanites (ID: `group:Canaan`); Zephath (ID: `place:Zephath`)

## Numbers 21:11–12 (id: 22765)

* The Israelites traveled along the east side of Edom.
* *Iye\-abarim* means "ruins of Abarim" or "ruins of the region beyond." It was probably near the northern bank of Zered Brook (compare [Deuteronomy 2:13–14](https://ref.ly/Deut2:13-Deut2:14)). This brook separated ancient Edom from Moab ([Deuteronomy 2:8–25](https://ref.ly/Deut2:8-Deut2:25)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 21:11-12

## Numbers 21:13 (id: 22766)

* The Israelites traveled east of the Moabite territory, located between the Zered Brook in the south and the Arnon River in the north. The far side of the Arnon River was probably north and east of the main east–west canyon of the Arnon. This huge canyon acted as a natural boundary between Moab and the Amorite territory north of the Arnon (see [21:26](https://ref.ly/Num21:26)).
* The Amorites were a group of nomadic (traveling) peoples. During the Middle Bronze Age (about 2000–1500 BC), they conquered areas from Mesopotamia to Canaan. They even ruled important cities such as Babylon (around 1830 BC) and Assur (around 1750 BC).

    Amorites living in the hill country of Canaan had frightened Israel’s spies ([Deuteronomy 1:26–28](https://ref.ly/Deut1:26-Deut1:28)). Later, they defeated Israel when the people tried to enter Canaan without God’s command ([Numbers 14:44–45](https://ref.ly/Num14:44-Num14:45); see [Deuteronomy 1:42–44](https://ref.ly/Deut1:42-Deut1:44)).

    When Israel arrived east of the Jordan River, the Amorites controlled much of that region. This area is called Transjordan, which means the land east of the Jordan (see study note on Numbers 21:26).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 21:13
* **Associated Articles:** Numbers 21:26 (ID: `22776@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Arnon (ID: `place:Arnon`); Amorites (ID: `group:Amorites`); Moab (ID: `place:Moab`)

## Numbers 21:14–15 (id: 22767)

* *the Book of the Wars of the LORD:* This is similar to The Book of Jashar ([Joshua 10:13](https://ref.ly/Josh10:13); [2 Samuel 1:18](https://ref.ly/2Sam1:18)). The Book of the Wars of the Lord is not known from any other source.
* Waheb, a town, and Suphah, a region, are not known from any other record. Their exact locations are unknown.

    Ar was one of the main settlements of Moab at that time (compare [21:28](https://ref.ly/Num21:28); [Deuteronomy 2:18](https://ref.ly/Deut2:18); [Isaiah 15:1](https://ref.ly/Isa15:1)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 21:14-15

## Numbers 21:16–18 (id: 22768)

The location of Beer is unknown (compare [Isaiah 15:8](https://ref.ly/Isa15:8)). The short poem in this passage describes the digging of a new well. It shows the joy the people felt. In a dry region, a new source of water was a great blessing.

The poem says that princes and leaders dug the well. It also says they used scepters and staffs, which were symbols of authority. This may suggest that the digging of the well was part of a special or joyful ceremony.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 21:16-18

## Numbers 21:18–20 (id: 22769)

After moving north of the Arnon, the Hebrews left the wilderness on the eastern side of Moab and the newly conquered Amorite territory (see study note on 21:21–35). They turned west toward the ridgeline that rises up from the Jordan Rift valley, then traveled north to Pisgah Peak (see also [23:14](https://ref.ly/Num23:14); [Deuteronomy 3:17](https://ref.ly/Deut3:17), [27](https://ref.ly/Deut3:27); [34:1](https://ref.ly/Deut34:1), [5](https://ref.ly/Deut34:5)).

## Numbers 21:21–24 (id: 22773)

King Sihon's land was between Moab and Ammon. God would not allow the Israelites to invade Ammon, Edom, or Moab ([Deuteronomy 2:19](https://ref.ly/Deut2:19); [Deuteronomy 2:4–9](https://ref.ly/Deut2:4-Deut2:9)). This was because they were relatives ([Genesis 19:30–38](https://ref.ly/Gen19:30-Gen19:38); [32:3](https://ref.ly/Gen32:3)). Yet, Israel needed to cross Transjordan (the region across the Jordan River) to enter Canaan. Sihon's territory was the natural route.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 21:21-24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Sihon (ID: `person:Sihon`); King’s Highway (ID: `place:KingsHighway`); Ammonites (ID: `group:Ammon`); Jahaz (ID: `place:Jahaz`); Dispossess (ID: `keyterm:Dispossess`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Ammon (ID: `place:Ammon`); Arnon (ID: `place:Arnon`); Jabbok (ID: `place:Jabbok`); Amorites (ID: `group:Amorites`); Sword (ID: `realia:Sword`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`)

## Numbers 21:21–35 (id: 22774)

* The Israelite victories over King Sihon of Heshbon and King Og of Bashan previewed the Hebrew conquest of Canaan. It came to represent God's promise to help his people when they were in need (see [13:10–12](https://ref.ly/Num13:10-Num13:12); [Deuteronomy 2:24–3:7](https://ref.ly/Deut2:24-Deut3:7); [Joshua 2:10](https://ref.ly/Josh2:10); [9:10](https://ref.ly/Josh9:10); [12:1–6](https://ref.ly/Josh12:1-Josh12:6); [Judges 11:19–22](https://ref.ly/Judg11:19-Judg11:22); [Nehemiah 9:22](https://ref.ly/Neh9:22); [Psalms 135:10–12](https://ref.ly/Ps135:10-Ps135:12); [136:17–22](https://ref.ly/Ps136:17-Ps136:22); [Jeremiah 48:45–46](https://ref.ly/Jer48:45-Jer48:46)).
* Israel had left the wilderness before reaching Pisgah (compare [Numbers 21:18](https://ref.ly/Num21:18), [23](https://ref.ly/Num21:23); [21:20](https://ref.ly/Num21:20)). So, this story probably indicates an earlier event before the movement in [21:18–20](https://ref.ly/Num21:18-Num21:20).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 21:21-35

## Numbers 21:25 (id: 22775)

The tribes of Reuben, Gad, and Manasseh settled in the area conquered at this time (compare chapter [32](https://ref.ly/Num32:1-Num32:42)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 21:25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Amorites (ID: `group:Amorites`); Heshbon (ID: `place:Heshbon`)

## Numbers 21:26 (id: 22776)

* Sihon king of the Amorites captured the area north of the Arnon from the Moabites, who controlled it earlier. Israel then occupied this region for several hundred years. King Mesha of Moab reclaimed it around 830 BC (see [2 Kings 3:4–27](https://ref.ly/2Kgs3:4-2Kgs3:27)). This came through a campaign detailed on the Mesha Inscription (also known as the Moabite Stone).
* Heshbon is usually considered to be Tell Hesban. No evidence yet has shown that people lived there in the Late Bronze Age (see study note on Numbers 21:1).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 21:26
* **Associated Articles:** Numbers 21:1 (ID: `22770@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moab (ID: `place:Moab`); Heshbon (ID: `place:Heshbon`); Arnon (ID: `place:Arnon`); Sihon (ID: `person:Sihon`); Amorites (ID: `group:Amorites`)

## Numbers 21:27–30 (id: 22777)

This ancient song may have been an old Amorite ballad about the victories of King Sihon. It may have celebrated his power and success.

Other interpreters believe that Israelite singers wrote the song later. In this view, it was a taunt song meant to mock Sihon’s defeat and celebrate Israel’s victory.

The flow of the story from [Numbers 21:26](https://ref.ly/Num21:26) to [21:27](https://ref.ly/Num21:27) supports the first view. Israel may have taken an older Amorite song and applied it to their own victory. Sihon and the Amorites had once defeated Moab. Now Israel had defeated them. Compare [Judges 11:12–28](https://ref.ly/Judg11:12-Judg11:28).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 21:27-30

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Heshbon (ID: `place:Heshbon`); Sihon (ID: `person:Sihon`); Moab (ID: `place:Moab`); Dibon (ID: `place:Dibon`); Medeba (ID: `place:Medeba`); Woe (ID: `keyterm:Woe`); Chemosh (ID: `deity:Chemosh`); Kenath (ID: `place:Kenath`); Ar (ID: `place:Ar`); Arnon (ID: `place:Arnon`); A Group of People (ID: `group:GenericGroup`); Bamoth-baal (ID: `place:Bamoth-baal`); Amorites (ID: `group:Amorites`)

## Numbers 22:1 (id: 22788)

After defeating the Amorite kingdoms, Israel settled in the plains of Moab ([21:21–35](https://ref.ly/Num21:21-Num21:35) and study note). This area is on the eastern side of the Jordan Valley, northeast of the Dead Sea, and across the river from Jericho's oasis. The Hebrews camped there until they crossed the Jordan River to enter Canaan ([Joshua 3:1](https://ref.ly/Josh3:1)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 22:1
* **Associated Articles:** Numbers 21:21-35 (ID: `22774@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Plains of Moab (ID: `place:MoabPlains`); Jericho (ID: `place:Jericho`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Jordan River (ID: `place:Jordan`)

## Numbers 22:2–3 (id: 22790)

There is no mention of Balak outside of this event ([Joshua 24:9](https://ref.ly/Josh24:9); [Judges 11:25](https://ref.ly/Judg11:25); [Micah 6:5](https://ref.ly/Mic6:5); [Revelation 2:14](https://ref.ly/Rev2:14)). Sihon, the Amorite king, had taken over his territory ([Numbers 21:26](https://ref.ly/Num21:26)). Israel had just defeated Sihon ([21:21–31](https://ref.ly/Num21:21-Num21:31)). Balak might have thought his small kingdom would be next.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 22:2-3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Balak (ID: `person:Balak`); Zippor (ID: `person:Zippor`); Moab (ID: `place:Moab`); Amorites (ID: `group:Amorites`)

## Numbers 22:2–24.25 (id: 22789)

The story of Balak and Balaam marks an important turning point in Israel’s history. It includes humor, drama, and deep teaching about God.

The story makes one main point clear. The Lord’s will must be carried out. No one could stop his plan for Israel.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 22:2-24:25

## Numbers 22:4 (id: 22801)

*Midian:* This desert people descended from Abraham ([Genesis 25:1–2](https://ref.ly/Gen25:1-Gen25:2); [Exodus 2:15–16](https://ref.ly/Exod2:15-Exod2:16)). Their agreement with Moab started their conflicts with Israel ([Numbers 22:7](https://ref.ly/Num22:7); see [25:6](https://ref.ly/Num25:6), [14–18](https://ref.ly/Num25:14-Num25:18); [31:2–10](https://ref.ly/Num31:2-Num31:10); [Judges 6–8](https://ref.ly/Judg6:1-Judg8:35)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 22:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Balak (ID: `person:Balak`); Zippor (ID: `person:Zippor`); Moabites (ID: `group:Moab`); Moab (ID: `place:Moab`); Midian (ID: `place:Midian`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`)

## Numbers 22:5–6 (id: 22802)

* Balaam was a pagan religious specialist called a *diviner*. A diviner was someone who claimed to receive messages from the spiritual world and predict the future. Balaam came from northwest Mesopotamia (see “Balaam” Profile).

    Like many ancient peoples, the nations of Moab and Midian believed that spoken words had power. They believed that a blessing or a curse spoken by a skilled diviner could shape future events. Diviners claimed they had special power to discover and influence what would happen next (see [Deuteronomy 18:14](https://ref.ly/Deut18:14); [1 Samuel 6:2](https://ref.ly/1Sam6:2); [2 Kings 21:6](https://ref.ly/2Kgs21:6); [Isaiah 44:25](https://ref.ly/Isa44:25); [Daniel 2:1–12](https://ref.ly/Dan2:1-Dan2:12), [27–28](https://ref.ly/Dan2:27-Dan2:28); [Micah 5:12](https://ref.ly/Mic5:12); [Zechariah 10:2](https://ref.ly/Zech10:2)).

    Israel’s enemies asked Balaam to place curses on the Hebrews. They believed that his spoken words would harm them. In the ancient world, armies often spoke curses against their enemies before battle.

    People in ancient Israel also believed that spoken words could have real effect, even when they were not part of divination (for example, [Genesis 27:1–40](https://ref.ly/Gen27:1-Gen27:40); [Deuteronomy 27:15–26](https://ref.ly/Deut27:15-Deut27:26); [1 Samuel 14:24–28](https://ref.ly/1Sam14:24-1Sam14:28); compare [Matthew 21:18–22](https://ref.ly/Matt21:18-Matt21:22)).

* Pethor is probably the ancient city of Pitru ([Numbers 22:5](https://ref.ly/Num22:5)). It was located on the west side of the Euphrates River, south of Carchemish, about 640 kilometers (400 miles) from Moab.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 22:5-6
* **Associated Articles:** Balaam (ID: `500473@TyndaleStudyNotesProfiles`)

## Numbers 22:18 (id: 22786)

Some people think Balaam answered the second group of messengers in a way that tried to gain a larger reward. They suggest that he wanted a more profitable offer.

However, Balaam later said that he could not go beyond the command of the Lord, even for a great reward ([24:13](https://ref.ly/Num24:13)). This statement makes that view less likely.

Instead, Balaam appeared determined to obey the Lord. He even called the Lord “my God,” which shows that he recognized the Lord’s authority.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 22:18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Balaam (ID: `person:Balaam`); Balak (ID: `person:Balak`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`)

## Numbers 22:41 (id: 22800)

*Bamoth\-baal* means "high places of Baal." It was on a ridge of hills above the plains of Moab, northeast of the Dead Sea (compare "Bamoth" in [21:19](https://ref.ly/Num21:19); [Joshua 13:17](https://ref.ly/Josh13:17)). It was the first of three places where the Moabite king took Balaam to view the large, spread\-out Israelite camp (also [Numbers 22:41](https://ref.ly/Num22:41); [23:14](https://ref.ly/Num23:14), [28](https://ref.ly/Num23:28)). From this high point, he could see the Israelites and perform ceremonies ([22:11](https://ref.ly/Num22:11)). Balak hoped these would result in a strong curse against them.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 22:41

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bamoth-baal (ID: `place:Bamoth-baal`); Balaam (ID: `person:Balaam`); Balak (ID: `person:Balak`)

## Numbers 23:1–30 (id: 22806)

* As a diviner, Balaam carried out rituals to look for signs about the future of Israel (compare [23:23](https://ref.ly/Num23:23); [24:1](https://ref.ly/Num24:1)). Balak had called him to curse Israel, but God repeatedly stopped him.
* These sacrifices might have related to *extispicy*. This was the practice of examining animal livers or other organs to predict the future. Ancient Near Eastern texts show that this fortune\-telling method was common and well\-developed. God had forbidden Israel from such practices (see [Deuteronomy 18:9–14](https://ref.ly/Deut18:9-Deut18:14); compare [Ezekiel 21:21](https://ref.ly/Ezek21:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 23:1-30

## Numbers 23:7–8 (id: 22815)

Instead of cursing Israel, Balaam spoke about their special status (compare [Exodus 19:5–6](https://ref.ly/Exod19:5-Exod19:6); [Deuteronomy 7:6–9](https://ref.ly/Deut7:6-Deut7:9)). He recognized that God's blessing had transformed a humble people into a great nation (compare [Genesis 12:2–3](https://ref.ly/Gen12:2-Gen12:3); [13:16](https://ref.ly/Gen13:16); [28:14](https://ref.ly/Gen28:14)). Balaam hoped for similar good fortune for himself ([Numbers 23:10](https://ref.ly/Num23:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 23:7-8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Curse (ID: `keyterm:Curse`); Balak (ID: `person:Balak`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Moab (ID: `place:Moab`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Paddan-aram (ID: `place:Paddan-aram`); Proverb (ID: `keyterm:Saying`)

## Numbers 23:7–10 (id: 22814)

* In his first message from God, Balaam reviewed why he had come to Moab ([23:5](https://ref.ly/Num23:5), [16](https://ref.ly/Num23:16)). He explained how Balak had called him. He also stressed that God had a special relationship with Israel. Balaam made clear that he could not harm Israel because God was protecting them.
* These messages are written as poetry. Hebrew poetry often uses parallelism. Parallelism places two similar lines together. The second line repeats or strengthens the first line in a clear and ordered way.
* *Aram:* This refers to Aram\-naharaim. This name describes North Mesopotamia, where Balaam’s hometown of Pethor was located ([Numbers 22:5](https://ref.ly/Num22:5)).
* *the mountains of the east*: This refers to a mountain range in North Syria.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 23:7-10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Curse (ID: `keyterm:Curse`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Balak (ID: `person:Balak`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Moab (ID: `place:Moab`); Paddan-aram (ID: `place:Paddan-aram`); Proverb (ID: `keyterm:Saying`)

## Numbers 23:13–26 (id: 22807)

Balak hoped for different results by changing the location. The exact location of the plateau of Zophim on Pisgah Peak is unknown. Yet, Pisgah is part of the ridge that is above the plains of Moab ([Deuteronomy 34:1](https://ref.ly/Deut34:1)). It is near Bamoth\-baal and Mount Peor ([Numbers 22:41](https://ref.ly/Num22:41); [23:28](https://ref.ly/Num23:28)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 23:13-26

Nothing would cause him to abandon his word or turn away from Israel.

Because God is faithful and always keeps his word, Balaam had no choice but to bless Israel.

He could not speak against them because God had decided to bless them.

## Numbers 23:20–21 (id: 22811)

The Lord kept his promises to bless his people, bringing them no misfortune or trouble. Balaam could not change God's plans. God punished the Hebrews for disobedience. Yet, he supported Israel's cause ever since he freed them from Egypt.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 23:20-21

## Numbers 23:23–24 (id: 22812)

No curse could overpower Israel because God blessed them ([Numbers 22:12](https://ref.ly/Num22:12); compare [Genesis 12:2–3](https://ref.ly/Gen12:2-Gen12:3); [22:17–18](https://ref.ly/Gen22:17-Gen22:18)). Israel was safe from the harm that Moab and Midian tried to cause through divination (compare [Numbers 24:1](https://ref.ly/Num24:1)). Israel would become famous because God protected them from harm (see [Joshua 2:9–11](https://ref.ly/Josh2:9-Josh2:11); compare [Numbers 14:13–19](https://ref.ly/Num14:13-Num14:19)).

## Numbers 23:27–24.14 (id: 22813)

Unhappy with Balaam's blessing, Balak hoped a new location would change the outcome. ## Numbers 24:1–2 (id: 22816)

Unlike before, Balaam did not use divination this time ([23:3](https://ref.ly/Num23:3), [15](https://ref.ly/Num23:15)). He already knew that signs and omens could not harm Israel ([23:23](https://ref.ly/Num23:23)). So he stopped looking for them. 

Instead, the Spirit of God came directly upon Balaam. God himself gave Balaam the message to speak.

## Numbers 24:6–7 (id: 22828)

The Lord’s favor is shown by comparing Israel to groves and gardens that have plenty of water. In a dry land, water brings life and growth. This picture shows how God was caring for his people.

Israel’s growing population also showed God’s blessing ([24:7](https://ref.ly/Num24:7); as mentioned in [23:10](https://ref.ly/Num23:10)). This growth would become even clearer in the second census recorded in chapter [26](https://ref.ly/Num26:1-Num26:65).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 24:6-7

## Numbers 24:7–9 (id: 22829)

* God's blessing would allow the Israelites defeat their enemies (see [24:15–24](https://ref.ly/Num24:15-Num24:24)).
* Agag was an Amalekite king whom King Saul defeated ([1 Samuel 15:7–9](https://ref.ly/1Sam15:7-1Sam15:9), [32–33](https://ref.ly/1Sam15:32-1Sam15:33)). The Amalekites, a desert people, were traditional enemies of the Hebrews (compare [Numbers 14:45](https://ref.ly/Num14:45); [Exodus 17:8–16](https://ref.ly/Exod17:8-Exod17:16)). Agag was probably a traditional title for their king. Since God brought Israel out of Egypt, he fought for them like a warrior and a wild animal (wild ox and lion; [Numbers 24:8–9](https://ref.ly/Num24:8-Num24:9)).
* *Blessed … cursed:* See [Genesis 12:3](https://ref.ly/Gen12:3); [27:29](https://ref.ly/Gen27:29).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 24:7-9

This prophecy gives more specific details than the first three messages. It is based on another vision from God (*the Most High*, [24:16](https://ref.ly/Num24:16)). It also predicts the appearance of a great leader who will play a major role in these military victories ([24:17](https://ref.ly/Num24:17)).

## Numbers 24:17 (id: 22820)

* *A star* symbolized a king (see [Isaiah 14:12](https://ref.ly/Isa14:12); [Matthew 2:1–10](https://ref.ly/Matt2:1-Matt2:10)). A *scepter* symbolized power and majesty (see [Genesis 49:10](https://ref.ly/Gen49:10); [Psalm 45:6](https://ref.ly/Ps45:6)).

    Historically, King David fits this description. The Moabites, whose defeat [Numbers 24:17](https://ref.ly/Num24:17) mentions, were among the many peoples he conquered during David's rule ([2 Samuel 8:2](https://ref.ly/2Sam8:2)). Yet, Moab remained a threat to Israel after David's time (see [2 Kings 3:1–27](https://ref.ly/2Kgs3:1-2Kgs3:27); [Isaiah 15–16](https://ref.ly/Isa15:1-Isa16:14); [Jeremiah 48](https://ref.ly/Jer48:1-Jer48:47)).

    Many interpreters have extended this image beyond David. Christians identify Jesus of Nazareth with *a star* and *a* *scepter* (compare [Matthew 2:2](https://ref.ly/Matt2:2); [2 Peter 1:19](https://ref.ly/2Pet1:19); [Revelation 2:28](https://ref.ly/Rev2:28); [22:16](https://ref.ly/Rev22:16); compare [Hebrews 1:8](https://ref.ly/Heb1:8)).

* *the sons of Sheth:* These people were probably the ancient Sutu, a group known from other records.

    However, the name may refer to a different people group. It could refer to:

    + Edom, also called Seir, which David later defeated ([Numbers 24:18](https://ref.ly/Num24:18)),
        + Ir, or Ar, a city of Moab ([24:19](https://ref.ly/Num24:19); compare [21:28](https://ref.ly/Num21:28)), or
        + Amalek, one of Israel’s earliest and most lasting enemies ([24:20](https://ref.ly/Num24:20)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 24:17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Moab (ID: `place:Moab`); Seth (ID: `person:Seth`)

## Numbers 24:21–22 (id: 22821)

Assyria would destroy the Kenites, a desert people living in the same areas as the Amalekites and Midianites. Moses married a Kenite ([Judges 1:16](https://ref.ly/Judg1:16); [4:11](https://ref.ly/Judg4:11); compare [Numbers 10:29–32](https://ref.ly/Num10:29-Num10:32)). The Kenites were mostly friendly with Israel after that time (for example, [1 Samuel 15:6](https://ref.ly/1Sam15:6); [27:10](https://ref.ly/1Sam27:10); [30:27–30](https://ref.ly/1Sam30:27-1Sam30:30)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 24:21-22

## Numbers 25:1 (id: 22834)

* *in Shittim:* From here, Joshua later sent spies across the Jordan River, and the Hebrews began their conquest ([Joshua 2:1](https://ref.ly/Josh2:1); [3:1](https://ref.ly/Josh3:1)).
* *daughters of Moab:* The Moabites and the Midianites both shared responsibility for what happened with Balaam ([Numbers 22:2–4](https://ref.ly/Num22:2-Num22:4), [7](https://ref.ly/Num22:7); [31:8](https://ref.ly/Num31:8), [15–16](https://ref.ly/Num31:15-Num31:16)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 25:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Shittim (ID: `place:Shittim`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Play the Prostitute (ID: `keyterm:PlayTheProstitute`); Moab (ID: `place:Moab`)

## Numbers 25:1–18 (id: 22830)

Israel was camped across the Jordan River from Jericho. They were almost ready to enter the land and begin the conquest. However, at this important moment, they fell into serious sin.

Balaam advised Israel’s enemies to lead the people into sexual immorality and idolatry at Baal\-peor ([31:16](https://ref.ly/Num31:16)). 

He could not harm Israel with a curse, so he chose another way. He led them into sin through lust and the worship of false gods.

The early church faced similar temptations. Believers were warned about idolatry and sexual immorality ([1 Corinthians 10:6](https://ref.ly/1Cor10:6), [11](https://ref.ly/1Cor10:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 25:1-18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Midian (ID: `place:Midian`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Midianites (ID: `group:Midian`); Plague (ID: `keyterm:Blow`); Cozbi (ID: `person:Cozbi`); Baal (ID: `deity:Baal`); Phinehas (ID: `person:Phinehas`); Be Zealous (ID: `keyterm:Zealous`); Eleazar (ID: `person:Eleazar.2`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); Salu (ID: `person:Salu`); Shittim (ID: `place:Shittim`); Zimri (ID: `person:Zimri.3`); Zur (ID: `person:Zur`); Simeon (ID: `person:Simeon.5`); Tribe of Simeon (ID: `group:Simeon`); Baal-peor (ID: `place:Peor`); Appease (ID: `keyterm:Appease`); Play the Prostitute (ID: `keyterm:PlayTheProstitute`); Priesthood (ID: `keyterm:Priesthood`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); Slaughtering (ID: `keyterm:Slaughtering`); Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); Moab (ID: `place:Moab`); Spear (ID: `realia:Spear`); Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`); Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`)

## Numbers 25:3 (id: 22836)

* *Baal of Peor* might refer to Chemosh by another name ([21:29](https://ref.ly/Num21:29)). More likely, it refers to *Baal*, which means “lord.” Baal was a Canaanite god. The Moabites honored several gods, and Baal was probably one of them.

    Many place names include the name Baal, such as Baal\-gad, Baal\-hermon, Baal\-meon, and Baal\-peor. These names likely marked local shrines where people worshiped Baal in different forms.

    Peor was a mountain from which Balaam could see the camp of Israel in the plains of Moab. Nearby was a shrine called Beth\-peor ([23:28](https://ref.ly/Num23:28); [25:3](https://ref.ly/Num25:3), [5](https://ref.ly/Num25:5), [18](https://ref.ly/Num25:18); [31:16](https://ref.ly/Num31:16); [Deuteronomy 3:29](https://ref.ly/Deut3:29); [4:3](https://ref.ly/Deut4:3), [46](https://ref.ly/Deut4:46); [34:6](https://ref.ly/Deut34:6); [Joshua 13:20](https://ref.ly/Josh13:20); [22:17](https://ref.ly/Josh22:17)).

* Israel’s worship of false gods made the Lord very angry. As a result, a plague broke out among the people ([Numbers 25:8–9](https://ref.ly/Num25:8-Num25:9); [26:1](https://ref.ly/Num26:1); compare[11:1](https://ref.ly/Num11:1), [10](https://ref.ly/Num11:10); [12:9](https://ref.ly/Num12:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 25:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Baal (ID: `deity:Baal`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Numbers 25:4 (id: 22837)

* As at Sinai, the Lord required quick justice ([Exodus 32:27–28](https://ref.ly/Exod32:27-Exod32:28)).
* *execute them in broad daylight:* They may have killed the guilty people and hung them on a pole. Then they would leave them in the sun so everyone could see and learn from their mistakes (see [2 Samuel 21:6](https://ref.ly/2Sam21:6), [9](https://ref.ly/2Sam21:9); compare [2 Samuel 12:12](https://ref.ly/2Sam12:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 25:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Numbers 25:6 (id: 22838)

* In this event, another Israelite man made himself unclean by being with a foreign woman. Both Moabite and Midianite women were involved in this scandal ([25:1–3](https://ref.ly/Num25:1-Num25:3), [16–18](https://ref.ly/Num25:16-Num25:18); [31:1–2](https://ref.ly/Num31:1-Num31:2)).

    His sin was especially bold. He acted without shame, even though people around him were mourning because of God’s judgment.

* Some interpreters suggest that his tent may have been more than his personal dwelling. They think it may have been an unauthorized shrine connected with the worship of Yahweh. If so, this would have made his sin even more serious.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 25:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Midianites (ID: `group:Midian`); Midian (ID: `place:Midian`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`)

## Numbers 25:7–9 (id: 22839)

Phinehas, acting as a representative of the priestly family, killed Zimri and Cozbi with one thrust of a spear ([25:7–8](https://ref.ly/Num25:7-Num25:8)). The text likely suggests that they were engaged in sexual relations at that moment.

Phinehas acted with zeal, which means strong passion to defend God’s honor. His action brought the plague to an end ([25:1–3](https://ref.ly/Num25:1-Num25:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 25:7-9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Plague (ID: `keyterm:Blow`); Phinehas (ID: `person:Phinehas`); Spear (ID: `realia:Spear`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Eleazar (ID: `person:Eleazar.2`); Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`)

## Numbers 25:10–13 (id: 22831)

Israel’s identity as the Lord’s people was in great danger because they were unfaithful at this time. Their sin threatened their special relationship with God.

Phinehas showed strong zeal (deep commitment and passion) for God’s holiness and justice. He acted in a way that matched God’s own concern for what is right. Because of this, God gave Phinehas and his descendants a special blessing.

God made a covenant of peace (a lasting promise of peace and favor) with Phinehas. The men in his family who were qualified could serve as priests forever (compare [Malachi 2:5](https://ref.ly/Mal2:5)). 

This covenant was unconditional (it did not depend on future actions). It was similar to the covenant God made with David (see [Jeremiah 33:19–22](https://ref.ly/Jer33:19-Jer33:22)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 25:10-13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be Zealous (ID: `keyterm:Zealous`); Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); Phinehas (ID: `person:Phinehas`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); Eleazar (ID: `person:Eleazar.2`); Appease (ID: `keyterm:Appease`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Priesthood (ID: `keyterm:Priesthood`)

## Numbers 25:13 (id: 22832)

The deaths of Zimri and Cozbi helped remove sin from the community of Israel. Their judgment stopped the spread of sin among the people.

Phinehas’s action had atoning value. This means it helped turn away God’s judgment and restore the people’s relationship with him. His act brought cleansing and peace to Israel (see study note on Leviticus 1:4).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 25:13
* **Associated Articles:** Leviticus 1:4 (ID: `20073@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Be Zealous (ID: `keyterm:Zealous`); Appease (ID: `keyterm:Appease`); Priesthood (ID: `keyterm:Priesthood`)

## Numbers 26:1–4 (id: 22840)

As in the first census, Moses and Eleazar directed the counting of the people. Eleazar had taken Aaron’s place as high priest.

They gave instructions to the tribal leaders about how to record the numbers of the men.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 26:1-4

## Numbers 26:1–65 (id: 22841)

After nearly 40 years, Israel registered their men of military age again. This was while camping on the plains of Moab across from Jericho (compare chapters [1–4](https://ref.ly/Num1:1-Num4:49)). The overall population changed only slightly, with 603,550 in the first registration and 601,730 in the second. This nearly complete replacement of the population showed that the Lord remained faithful to Israel despite their repeated acts of rebellion ([26:63–65](https://ref.ly/Num26:63-Num26:65)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 26:1-65

## Numbers 26:52–56 (id: 22844)

* Moses counted the Levites separately (compare [1:47–49](https://ref.ly/Num1:47-Num1:49); [3:14–39](https://ref.ly/Num3:14-Num3:39)).

Instead, they received 48 towns for their use, but no large territory as described in [26:52–56](https://ref.ly/Num26:52-Num26:56). ([35:1–8](https://ref.ly/Num35:1-Num35:8); compare [18:23–24](https://ref.ly/Num18:23-Num18:24)).

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); To Cause to Possess (ID: `keyterm:Possess`)

## Numbers 26:63–65 (id: 22847)

The numbers in this second registration describe a completely new generation of Hebrews. Everyone who had left Egypt had died in the wilderness except Caleb and Joshua.

Joshua became the leader of Israel after Moses ([27:12–23](https://ref.ly/Num27:12-Num27:23); see “Joshua” Profile). Caleb also later played an important role in Israel’s history (see “Caleb” Profile). 

[Numbers 26](https://ref.ly/Num26:1-Num26:65) shows an important change. The earlier generation had lived under God’s judgment because of their unbelief (chapter [14](https://ref.ly/Num14:1-Num14:45)). Now a new generation stood ready for the future, just as Balaam’s four prophecies had promised (chapters [23–24](https://ref.ly/Num23:1-Num24:25)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 26:63-65
* **Associated Articles:** Joshua (ID: `500475@TyndaleStudyNotesProfiles`); Caleb (ID: `500477@TyndaleStudyNotesProfiles`)

## Numbers 27:2 (id: 22855)

*the entrance of the Tent of Meeting*: This was the most important public place, where leaders made important decisions. [Joshua 17:4](https://ref.ly/Josh17:4) specifically mentions Eleazar, Joshua, and the Hebrew leaders. But it does not mention Moses. These women asked for an exception to the existing law and presented their case to the highest tribal authorities.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 27:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Eleazar (ID: `person:Eleazar.2`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`)

## Numbers 27:3–4 (id: 22856)

Zelophehad had not joined Korah’s rebellion ([16:1–50](https://ref.ly/Num16:1-Num16:50)).If he had taken part in that rebellion, he may have lost his property rights because of his sin (see [15:30](https://ref.ly/Num15:30); compare [1 Kings 21:7–16](https://ref.ly/1Kgs21:7-1Kgs21:16)). 

Israelite society was patriarchal and patrilineal. This means that family ancestry and property were passed down through the male line. Normally, sons inherited their father’s land.

Zelophehad had no sons. His daughters wanted their father’s land to remain in their family. They asked for property rights equal to those given to sons. Their request was reasonable and based on a desire to preserve their family’s inheritance.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 27:3-4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Korah (ID: `person:Korah.3`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Numbers 27:5–11 (id: 22857)

Moses brought their case before the Lord. The Lord accepted the women’s request.

God then established their case as a general rule. This new rule helped preserve property within families when there were no sons to inherit it.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 27:5-11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Cause to Possess (ID: `keyterm:Possess`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Zelophehad (ID: `person:Zelophehad`); Dispossess (ID: `keyterm:Dispossess`)

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 27:12

## Numbers 27:15–17 (id: 22852)

Israel was about to begin a long battle for Canaan. Moses did not want them to struggle without a leader or become lost like sheep without a shepherd (compare [1 Kings 22:17](https://ref.ly/1Kgs22:17); [Ezekiel 34:5](https://ref.ly/Ezek34:5); [Matthew 9:36](https://ref.ly/Matt9:36)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 27:15-17
