## Numbers 27:18–19 (id: 22853)

* Joshua was well prepared to become Israel’s leader. He had already led Israel in battle (compare [Exodus 17:8–16](https://ref.ly/Exod17:8-Exod17:16)). He had also served as Moses’s assistant for many years ([Numbers 11:28](https://ref.ly/Num11:28); compare [Exodus 33:11](https://ref.ly/Exod33:11)).

    Joshua was one of the two faithful spies who knew the people of Israel well ([Numbers 13–14](https://ref.ly/Num13:1-Num14:45)). The Spirit of God was with Joshua (compare [11:25–29](https://ref.ly/Num11:25-Num11:29)). His close relationship with God gave him wisdom for the work ahead (for example, [Deuteronomy 34:9](https://ref.ly/Deut34:9)).

* *lay your hands on him:* This act symbolized transferring authority from an elder leader to his assistant (compare [Numbers 8:10–14](https://ref.ly/Num8:10-Num8:14)). Moses did this publicly so that no one would question Joshua's leadership.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 27:18-19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Joshua (ID: `person:Joshua.2`); Nun (ID: `person:Nun`); Eleazar (ID: `person:Eleazar.2`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`)

## Numbers 27:20–21 (id: 22854)

Joshua received only part of the authority Moses had. Moses had spoken with God face to face ([12:6–8](https://ref.ly/Num12:6-Num12:8); [Deuteronomy 34:10–12](https://ref.ly/Deut34:10-Deut34:12)). Joshua did not have that same direct access to God’s revelation.

Instead, Joshua had to seek guidance through Eleazar the priest on important matters ([Numbers 27:21](https://ref.ly/Num27:21); compare [Exodus 28:30](https://ref.ly/Exod28:30); [Leviticus 8:8](https://ref.ly/Lev8:8)). Eleazar used the priestly means of seeking God’s will.

From this time on, Israel separated military leadership from spiritual leadership. Joshua led the army, while the priest guided the people in spiritual matters.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 27:20-21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Eleazar (ID: `person:Eleazar.2`); Majesty (ID: `keyterm:Majesty`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Numbers 28:1–2 (id: 22859)

The Lord accepted special gifts *as a pleasing aroma* (compare [15:3](https://ref.ly/Num15:3)). This means God accepted and enjoyed these gifts (see also [Genesis 8:21](https://ref.ly/Gen8:21); [Leviticus 1:9](https://ref.ly/Lev1:9); [Ephesians 5:2](https://ref.ly/Eph5:2); [Philippians 4:18](https://ref.ly/Phil4:18)). The priests made offerings at set times following specific instructions (see [Numbers 28:3–29:39](https://ref.ly/Num28:3-Num29:39)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 28:1-2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Pleasing Odor (ID: `keyterm:PleasingOdor`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`)

## Numbers 28:1–29.40 (id: 22858)

* These two chapters describe Israel's ceremonial calendar. It listed daily, weekly, and monthly offerings, along with annual religious events. God instructed Israel to worship him "in a proper and orderly manner" ([1 Corinthians 14:40](https://ref.ly/1Cor14:40)).

    The annual sacrifices required many animals, grain, oil, and wine. On these special days, Israel stopped their usual work. This was a sacrifice that emphasized the importance of these holy days.

    The annual cycle included eight different occasions for worship, each reflecting Israel's relationship with the Lord. Each event involved different combinations of burnt, grain, liquid, or sin offerings.

* For details on grain and liquid offerings, see [Numbers 15:1–16](https://ref.ly/Num15:1-Num15:16). For explanations of different offerings, see [Leviticus 1:1–7:38](https://ref.ly/Lev1:1-Lev7:38). For a description of Israel's festivals, see [Leviticus 23:1–44](https://ref.ly/Lev23:1-Lev23:44). For the New Testament view on the Old Testament sacrificial system, see [John 1:29](https://ref.ly/John1:29); [Romans 12:1](https://ref.ly/Rom12:1); [Hebrews 9:9–15](https://ref.ly/Heb9:9-Heb9:15); [10:1–2](https://ref.ly/Heb10:1-Heb10:2); [1 Peter 2:5](https://ref.ly/1Pet2:5).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 28:1-29:40

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Wine (ID: `realia:Wine`); Complete (ID: `keyterm:Complete`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Pleasing Odor (ID: `keyterm:PleasingOdor`); Work (ID: `keyterm:Work`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Olive Oil (ID: `realia:OliveOil`); Anointing Oil (ID: `realia:AnointingOil`); Appease (ID: `keyterm:Appease`); Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Hin (ID: `realia:Hin`); Atonement (ID: `keyterm:Atonement`); Vow (ID: `keyterm:Vow`); Mount Sinai (ID: `place:SinaiMount`); Passover (ID: `keyterm:Passover`); Feast of Weeks (ID: `keyterm:FeastOfWeeks`); Holy Place (ID: `place:HolyPlace`); Appointed Feast (ID: `keyterm:AppointedFeast`); Unleavened Bread (ID: `realia:UnleavenedBread`); Mule (ID: `fauna:Mule`); Ephah (ID: `realia:Ephah`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`)

## Numbers 28:9–10 (id: 22865)

Special days required extra offerings. On the Sabbath, people made a sacrifice in addition to the regular burnt offering and its liquid offering. Other passages emphasize the importance of the Sabbath (for example, [Exodus 20:8–11](https://ref.ly/Exod20:8-Exod20:11); [Leviticus 23:3](https://ref.ly/Lev23:3); [Deuteronomy 5:12–15](https://ref.ly/Deut5:12-Deut5:15)).

## Numbers 28:11–15 (id: 22860)

The first day of each month was the *new moon*. On that day, the people sacrificed bulls, rams, and lambs as burnt offerings. They also sacrificed a male goat as a sin offering (an offering for forgiveness of sin).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 28:11-15

## Numbers 28:16–25 (id: 22861)

The Passover, followed by the Feast of Unleavened Bread, was the first of five major annual feasts. It took place in early spring (March or April). The Passover did not require public sacrifices. It was a family event celebrated at home (see also [9:1–4](https://ref.ly/Num9:1-Num9:4); [Exodus 12:1–13:10](https://ref.ly/Exod12:1-Exod13:10); [Leviticus 23:5–8](https://ref.ly/Lev23:5-Lev23:8); [Deuteronomy 16:1–8](https://ref.ly/Deut16:1-Deut16:8)). 

This festival is important in the background of the Last Supper (see [Matthew 26:17–19](https://ref.ly/Matt26:17-Matt26:19), [26–27](https://ref.ly/Matt26:26-Matt26:27); [Mark 14:12](https://ref.ly/Mark14:12); [John 11:55](https://ref.ly/John11:55); [12:1](https://ref.ly/John12:1); [13:1](https://ref.ly/John13:1); [18:28](https://ref.ly/John18:28), [39](https://ref.ly/John18:39); [19:14](https://ref.ly/John19:14), [31](https://ref.ly/John19:31); compare [1 Corinthians 5:7](https://ref.ly/1Cor5:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 28:16-25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`); Work (ID: `keyterm:Work`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Pleasing Odor (ID: `keyterm:PleasingOdor`); Passover (ID: `keyterm:Passover`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Appease (ID: `keyterm:Appease`); Complete (ID: `keyterm:Complete`); Wine (ID: `realia:Wine`); Anointing Oil (ID: `realia:AnointingOil`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Unleavened Bread (ID: `realia:UnleavenedBread`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`); Olive Oil (ID: `realia:OliveOil`)

## Numbers 28:26–31 (id: 22862)

The Feast of Weeks was later called the Feast of Pentecost (see [Acts 2:1](https://ref.ly/Acts2:1); also [Leviticus 23:15–22](https://ref.ly/Lev23:15-Lev23:22)). It marked the end of the barley harvest and the start of the wheat harvest in early summer (May or June). The Greek name *Pentecost* means “fiftieth.” It refers to the fiftieth day after Passover (see [Acts 20:16](https://ref.ly/Acts20:16); [1 Corinthians 16:8](https://ref.ly/1Cor16:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 28:26-31

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Appease (ID: `keyterm:Appease`); Work (ID: `keyterm:Work`); Pleasing Odor (ID: `keyterm:PleasingOdor`); Feast of Weeks (ID: `keyterm:FeastOfWeeks`); Complete (ID: `keyterm:Complete`); Wine (ID: `realia:Wine`); Anointing Oil (ID: `realia:AnointingOil`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Olive Oil (ID: `realia:OliveOil`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`)

## Numbers 29:7 (id: 22870)

*On the tenth day of this seventh month:* Today this day is known as Yom Kippur. The name comes from the Hebrew *yom hakkippurim*, which means “day of atonement” (a day for seeking forgiveness for sin, [Leviticus 23:27](https://ref.ly/Lev23:27)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 29:7


## Numbers 29:7–11 (id: 22869)

The Day of Atonement was a serious and holy day. It was a day of fasting, when the people did not eat food as a sign of repentance and prayer (see also [Leviticus 16](https://ref.ly/Lev16:1-Lev16:34); [23:26–32](https://ref.ly/Lev23:26-Lev23:32); [Romans 3:23–25](https://ref.ly/Rom3:23-Rom3:25); [Hebrews 9:7–12](https://ref.ly/Heb9:7-Heb9:12), [23–28](https://ref.ly/Heb9:23-Heb9:28)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 29:7-11

## Numbers 29:12–38 (id: 22867)

The Feast of Tabernacles began on the fifteenth day of the seventh month. It was an eight\-day harvest celebration that required many offerings ([Numbers 29:12](https://ref.ly/Num29:12), [35](https://ref.ly/Num29:35); [Exodus 34:22](https://ref.ly/Exod34:22)). This festival was a joyful time for giving thanks (see [Nehemiah 8:13–18](https://ref.ly/Neh8:13-Neh8:18)). The tabernacles (or shelters) reminded the Israelites of living in tents during their time in the wilderness (see also [Leviticus 23:33–43](https://ref.ly/Lev23:33-Lev23:43); [1 Kings 8:1–2](https://ref.ly/1Kgs8:1-1Kgs8:2)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 29:12-38

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Wine (ID: `realia:Wine`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Complete (ID: `keyterm:Complete`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Work (ID: `keyterm:Work`); Pleasing Odor (ID: `keyterm:PleasingOdor`); Anointing Oil (ID: `realia:AnointingOil`); Olive Oil (ID: `realia:OliveOil`)

## Numbers 30:1–2 (id: 22885)

Vows included promises to give offerings, like Hannah's vow in [1 Samuel 1](https://ref.ly/1Sam1:1-1Sam1:28). It included pledges to abstain from certain things, like the Nazirite vow in [Numbers 6](https://ref.ly/Num6:1-Num6:27). Jesus mentioned the teachings about vows in the Sermon on the Mount in [Matthew 5:33](https://ref.ly/Matt5:33).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 30:1-2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Vow (ID: `keyterm:Vow`); Swear (ID: `keyterm:Swear`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); To Dishonor (ID: `keyterm:Dishonor`); Oath (ID: `keyterm:Oath`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`)

## Numbers 30:1–16 (id: 22884)

Vows were an important way to show devotion or piety. But people sometimes made them quickly and forgot them. People often made voluntary pledges during special religious events or in times of war. The mention of vows and voluntary offerings might have led to further discussion here ([29:39](https://ref.ly/Num29:39); compare [15:3](https://ref.ly/Num15:3); see also [6:1–21](https://ref.ly/Num6:1-Num6:21); [Leviticus 5:4–5](https://ref.ly/Lev5:4-Lev5:5); [27:1–34](https://ref.ly/Lev27:1-Lev27:34); [Deuteronomy 23:21–23](https://ref.ly/Deut23:21-Deut23:23)). 

These instructions applied to Hannah and Elkanah ([1 Samuel 1:1–28](https://ref.ly/1Sam1:1-1Sam1:28)). Such practices, including fasting, continued into the New Testament period (for example, [Matthew 6:16](https://ref.ly/Matt6:16); [15:3–9](https://ref.ly/Matt15:3-Matt15:9); [Acts 18:18](https://ref.ly/Acts18:18); [21:23](https://ref.ly/Acts21:23)). Vows made by females needed approval from the responsible male who would share financial responsibility for the vow. This applied to young and unmarried or adult and married women. No one, male or female, young or old, should make a vow to God and then ignore it.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 30:1-16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Vow (ID: `keyterm:Vow`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); Oath (ID: `keyterm:Oath`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Swear (ID: `keyterm:Swear`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Decree (ID: `keyterm:Decree`); To Dishonor (ID: `keyterm:Dishonor`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); Bear the Consequences Of (ID: `keyterm:BearTheConsequencesOf`)

## Numbers 30:3–5 (id: 22887)

A young woman was under her father's authority. He was responsible for her actions and financial obligations.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 30:3-5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Vow (ID: `keyterm:Vow`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`)

## Numbers 30:6–8 (id: 22888)

When a young Hebrew woman married, her husband gained authority over her. He could cancel any vow or pledge she made while under her father's authority. This was only true if he canceled it as soon as he heard about it.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 30:6-8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Vow (ID: `keyterm:Vow`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Numbers 30:9 (id: 22889)

A widow or divorced woman usually returned to her father's family ([Leviticus 22:13](https://ref.ly/Lev22:13)). Yet, a mature single woman who made a vow or pledge had to fulfill her religious duties.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 30:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Vow (ID: `keyterm:Vow`)

## Numbers 30:10–15 (id: 22886)

A married woman needed her husband's approval to make vows and pledges. Her husband could cancel her religious promise. But he had to decide on the day he heard about it. God did not blame the married woman for breaking the promise if her husband canceled it correctly (compare [30:5](https://ref.ly/Num30:5), [8](https://ref.ly/Num30:8), [12](https://ref.ly/Num30:12)). 

The self\-denial mentioned in [30:13](https://ref.ly/Num30:13) probably refers to fasting. If he did not object, the husband's silence meant he agreed. He could not delay his objection, or he would be guilty of a broken vow (compare [Leviticus 5:4–13](https://ref.ly/Lev5:4-Lev5:13)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 30:10-15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Vow (ID: `keyterm:Vow`); Oath (ID: `keyterm:Oath`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); Bear the Consequences Of (ID: `keyterm:BearTheConsequencesOf`)

## Numbers 31:1–2 (id: 22890)

The Lord instructed Moses to lead Israel in a war against the Midianites. The Midianites had joined the Moabites in influencing the Hebrews to worship idols (chapter [25](https://ref.ly/Num25:1-Num25:18)). The Midianites were a tribal people living in the dry regions south and east of Palestine and east of the Jordan River. They were among the "people of the east" ([Judges 6:3](https://ref.ly/Judg6:3), [33](https://ref.ly/Judg6:33); [7:12](https://ref.ly/Judg7:12)). They lived in camps or settlements and were famous for their desert transportation skills (compare [Genesis 37:28](https://ref.ly/Gen37:28), [36](https://ref.ly/Gen37:36); [Isaiah 60:6](https://ref.ly/Isa60:6)). 

They descended from Abraham and had been friendly to Moses ([Genesis 25:1–2](https://ref.ly/Gen25:1-Gen25:2); [Exodus 2:15–3:1](https://ref.ly/Exod2:15-Exod3:1); compare [Numbers 10:29](https://ref.ly/Num10:29)). Yet, the elders of Midian joined Balak in hiring Balaam to curse Israel ([22:4](https://ref.ly/Num22:4), [7](https://ref.ly/Num22:7)). Israel's presence threatened the peoples of Transjordan (the area east of the Jordan River).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 31:1-2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Midianites (ID: `group:Midian`); Midian (ID: `place:Midian`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`)

## Numbers 31:3–5 (id: 22897)

Each tribe sent 1,000 men to the battle. This allowed everyone to gain combat experience and share the victory.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 31:3-5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Midian (ID: `place:Midian`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Numbers 31:6 (id: 22902)

* Phinehas, son of Eleazar the high priest, led this force of 12,000 men into battle. His leadership showed that this battle was considered a “holy war” (a war carried out in obedience to God). It was an act of judgment for the spiritual harm that Midian had caused Israel (see [25:6–18](https://ref.ly/Num25:6-Num25:18); [31:15–16](https://ref.ly/Num31:15-Num31:16)). His zeal in this situation matched his actions in the case of Zimri and Cozbi ([Numbers 25:11](https://ref.ly/Num25:11), [13](https://ref.ly/Num25:13); [25:7–8](https://ref.ly/Num25:7-Num25:8)).
* Many scholars believe the holy objects of the holy place included the ark of the covenant, which the Hebrews sometimes carried into battle (for example, [Numbers 10:35–36](https://ref.ly/Num10:35-Num10:36); [Joshua 6:6](https://ref.ly/Josh6:6); [1 Samuel 4:3–11](https://ref.ly/1Sam4:3-1Sam4:11); see also [Numbers 14:44](https://ref.ly/Num14:44)). Other scholars suggest they were various holy objects used in Tent of Meeting ceremonies ([3:31](https://ref.ly/Num3:31); [4:15](https://ref.ly/Num4:15); [18:3](https://ref.ly/Num18:3); see also [1 Kings 8:4](https://ref.ly/1Kgs8:4); [1 Chronicles 9:29](https://ref.ly/1Chr9:29); [2 Chronicles 5:5](https://ref.ly/2Chr5:5)). The Israelites likely took some or all of these holy objects as signs that God was with them in this battle.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 31:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Eleazar (ID: `person:Eleazar.2`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Phinehas (ID: `person:Phinehas`); Trumpet (ID: `realia:Trumpet`)

## Numbers 31:7 (id: 22903)

The Israelites killed all of the Midianite men in the particular groups they attacked from their camp in the plains of Moab ([31:12](https://ref.ly/Num31:12)). They did not destroy all the Midianites who were part of the larger Midianite alliance (compare [Judges 6–8](https://ref.ly/Judg6:1-Judg8:35)). The number of animals and virgins mentioned in [Numbers 31:32–35](https://ref.ly/Num31:32-Num31:35) shows that the 12,000 Hebrews fought a large enemy army.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 31:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Midian (ID: `place:Midian`)

Balaam son of Beor was put to death because his advice caused the people of Israel to turn against the Lord at Mount Peor([Numbers 31:16](https://ref.ly/Num31:16)). He was judged for his evil actions (compare [2 Peter 2:15](https://ref.ly/2Pet2:15); [Jude 1:11](https://ref.ly/Jude1:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 31:8

## Numbers 31:9–13 (id: 22905)

After the battle, the Hebrews began the practice that they sometimes followed throughout the period of conquest and beyond (for example, [Joshua 6](https://ref.ly/Josh6:1-Josh6:27); [8](https://ref.ly/Josh8:1-Josh8:35); [10–11](https://ref.ly/Josh10:1-Josh11:23)). They destroyed their enemies (sometimes the total population), burned towns, and set aside spoils for their own use or as dedicated to the Lord.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 31:9-13

## Numbers 31:19–24 (id: 22895)

Israelite soldiers who touched dead bodies became ceremonially unclean (ritually impure). They had to go through the required purification rituals.

These rituals were routine, but they were important. They also had to purify their equipment (see [5:1–4](https://ref.ly/Num5:1-Num5:4); [19:1–22](https://ref.ly/Num19:1-Num19:22)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 31:19-24

## Numbers 31:25–31 (id: 22896)

They divided the plunder into two equal parts. Half went to those who fought against the Midianites and half to the Israelites who stayed behind (compare [1 Samuel 30:21–25](https://ref.ly/1Sam30:21-1Sam30:25)). In this way, everyone participated in the conflict. Yet, the smaller group of soldiers each received a larger portion than the much larger group who stayed behind.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 31:25-31

## Numbers 31:48–49 (id: 22900)

It is unusual for an army to lose no soldiers in battle, but it is not impossible. Ancient writers also described battles in which few or no Roman soldiers were killed.

If the Israelites were well prepared and attacked by surprise, and if they had many more soldiers than their enemies, they could have won the battle without losing any men. This would be even more likely because of God’s blessing.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 31:48-49

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Appoint (ID: `keyterm:Appoint`)

## Numbers 31:50–54 (id: 22901)

The commanders happily offered gold objects to show gratitude for a great victory with no loss of life (see [Exodus 30:12](https://ref.ly/Exod30:12)). Ancient art and literature show that the Midianites and other desert peoples, both men and women, wore gold jewelry. They used it as decoration, and as a form of wealth they could take with them (for example, [Judges 8:24–26](https://ref.ly/Judg8:24-Judg8:26)). 

Moses and Eleazar brought this large collection to the Tent of Meeting as a memorial of this special event (compare [Numbers 16:39–40](https://ref.ly/Num16:39-Num16:40); [Zechariah 6:14](https://ref.ly/Zech6:14)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 31:50-54

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Eleazar (ID: `person:Eleazar.2`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Appease (ID: `keyterm:Appease`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`); Earring (ID: `realia:Earring`); Shekel (ID: `realia:Shekel`)

## Numbers 32:1 (id: 22909)

* *very large herds and flocks:* The Israelites had flocks and herds when they left Egypt (see [Exodus 12:38](https://ref.ly/Exod12:38)). They gained more animals as plunder in the war with Midian (see [Numbers 31:32–33](https://ref.ly/Num31:32-Num31:33)). They might have also gained it in their victories over Sihon and Og (see [21:21–35](https://ref.ly/Num21:21-Num21:35)).
* *Gilead* can refer to either the entire northern region of the plateau east of the Jordan River (for example, [32:29](https://ref.ly/Num32:29)). It can also refer only to the land between the Jabbok River and the Arnon River.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 32:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Gad (ID: `person:Gad`); Reuben (ID: `person:Reuben`); Gilead (ID: `place:Gilead`); Tribe of Reuben (ID: `group:Reuben`); Jazer (ID: `place:Jazer`)

## Numbers 32:1–5 (id: 22906)

After Israel took control of the central area east of the Jordan River, during the battles described in chapter [21](https://ref.ly/Num21:1-Num21:35), they began preparing to enter Canaan.

At that time, the Israelites needed to remain united. But two tribes asked for permission to settle on the east side of the Jordan River instead of crossing into Canaan.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 32:1-5

## Numbers 32:3 (id: 22918)

* Reuben, Gad, and half of Manasseh settled in the land Israel had recently captured from Sihon the Amorite and Og of Bashan (see [21:21–35](https://ref.ly/Num21:21-Num21:35)).
* Ataroth, Dibon, Jazer, Nimrah, Heshbon, and Elealeh have been connected with known archaeological sites. These places have been identified with ruins that researchers have studied.
* The location of Sebam is unknown.
* The exact location of the town called Nebo is not known. However, it was likely near a mountain or ridge with the same name. This mountain stood along the steep slope (escarpment) that rises above the northern end of the Dead Sea.
* The place called Beon is almost certainly the same as “Baal\-meon” in [32:38\.](https://ref.ly/Num32:38) It likely matches the ruins at Ma’in.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 32:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ataroth (ID: `place:Ataroth`); Beth-nimrah (ID: `place:Beth-nimrah`); Baal-meon (ID: `place:Baal-meon`); Dibon (ID: `place:Dibon`); Sebam (ID: `place:Sebam`); Elealeh (ID: `place:Elealeh`); Heshbon (ID: `place:Heshbon`); Nebo (ID: `place:Nebo`); Jazer (ID: `place:Jazer`)

## Numbers 32:6–15 (id: 22919)

Moses assumed that Reuben and Gad wanted to stay east of the Jordan River to avoid the dangers that awaited the other tribes in Canaan (compare chapters [13–14](https://ref.ly/Num13:1-Num14:45)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 32:6-15

## Numbers 32:12 (id: 22907)

Caleb is identified as a Kenizzite, a descendant of Esau (see [Genesis 36:10–11](https://ref.ly/Gen36:10-Gen36:11)). This suggests that Caleb was not born as an Israelite. He may have been a convert (someone who joined Israel’s faith). Even so, he became fully part of Israel’s life and worship.

Later, Caleb represented the tribe of Judah when Moses sent out the twelve spies to explore the promised land ([Numbers 13:6](https://ref.ly/Num13:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 32:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jephunneh (ID: `person:Jephunneh`); Kenizzites (ID: `group:Kenaz`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Joshua (ID: `person:Joshua.2`); Nun (ID: `person:Nun`); Caleb (ID: `person:Caleb`); Kenaz (ID: `person:Kenaz.3`)

## Numbers 32:16–19 (id: 22908)

The Reubenites and Gadites assured Moses that they would not discourage their fellow Israelites and would support the war to its very end. There were still dangers east of the Jordan River, so they wanted to keep their livestock and families safe.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 32:16-19

## Numbers 32:20–24 (id: 22910)

Moses agreed to the terms offered by Reuben and Gad. If they helped their relatives fight the Canaanites, they would fulfill their part of the agreement. Then they could return to their settlements east of the Jordan River ([32:22](https://ref.ly/Num32:22)). If they did not keep their promise, it would be a serious sin against God ([32:23](https://ref.ly/Num32:23)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 32:20-24

## Numbers 32:33–42 (id: 22914)

* The tribes of Gad and Reuben, along with half the tribe of Manasseh, settled east of the Jordan River (for example, [Deuteronomy 3:12–13](https://ref.ly/Deut3:12-Deut3:13); [4:43](https://ref.ly/Deut4:43); [29:7–8](https://ref.ly/Deut29:7-Deut29:8)). These tribes lived in lands and towns captured from King Sihon the Amorite and King Og of Bashan ([Numbers 32:33](https://ref.ly/Num32:33); see [21:21–35](https://ref.ly/Num21:21-Num21:35)).
* Half the tribe of Manasseh suddenly appears in the discussion about distributing the land. Part of Manasseh's tribe settled east of the Jordan River, while another part settled in Canaan, west of the Jordan (compare [Joshua 17:1–12](https://ref.ly/Josh17:1-Josh17:12); [22:7](https://ref.ly/Josh22:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 32:33-42

## Numbers 32:37–38 (id: 22916)

## Numbers 33:1–2 (id: 22920)

The list of travel places shows careful record\-keeping. It includes many specific details. This is the only place in Numbers that says Moses wrote down part of Israel’s history (compare [Exodus 24:4](https://ref.ly/Exod24:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 33:1-2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)

## Numbers 33:1–56 (id: 22921)

* This review of the entire wilderness period includes the longest connected list of place names in the Old Testament. It begins with Israel’s departure from Egypt ([33:3](https://ref.ly/Num33:3); compare [Exodus 12:37](https://ref.ly/Exod12:37)). It ends with their arrival in the plains of Moab, across from Jericho ([Numbers 33:49](https://ref.ly/Num33:49); compare [22:1](https://ref.ly/Num22:1)).

    The list names 42 stopping places along the way. These places represent more than a physical journey from one land to another. They also remind readers of Israel’s forty years of spiritual growth and testing.

    Israel traveled from Rameses in Egypt ([33:3](https://ref.ly/Num33:3)) to Abel\-shittim on the plains of Moab ([33:49](https://ref.ly/Num33:49)). During these years, the people became ready to enter the land of Canaan and receive the promises God had made to Abraham.

* This list does not give enough information to trace an exact route on a map. Many of the places cannot be identified with certainty. Some of these names do not appear anywhere else in the Hebrew Bible.

    In addition, there are not enough details to locate these places precisely. The list is also selective. It does not include every place that earlier parts of the journey mention.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 33:1-56

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Mount Hor (ID: `place:HorMount`); Jordan River (ID: `place:Jordan`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Dispossess (ID: `keyterm:Dispossess`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Plains of Moab (ID: `place:MoabPlains`); Etham (ID: `place:Etham`); Terah (ID: `place:Terah`); Rimmon-perez (ID: `place:Rimmon-perez`); Iye-abarim (ID: `place:Iye-abarim`); Haradah (ID: `place:Haradah`); Libnah (ID: `place:Libnah`); Makheloth (ID: `place:Makheloth`); Mithkah (ID: `place:Mithkah`); Dophkah (ID: `place:Dophkah`); Moseroth (ID: `place:Moseroth`); Ezion-geber (ID: `place:Ezion-geber`); Punon (ID: `place:Punon`); Zalmonah (ID: `place:Zalmonah`); Hashmonah (ID: `place:Hashmonah`); Rissah (ID: `place:Rissah`); Hor-haggidgad (ID: `place:Hor-haggidgad`); Jotbathah (ID: `place:Jotbathah`); Almon-diblathaim (ID: `place:Almon-diblathaim`); Kehelathah (ID: `place:Kehelathah`); Rithmah (ID: `place:Rithmah`); Mount Nebo (ID: `place:NeboMount`)

Many possible locations for these places are east of the Jordan River. They lie between the southern Arabah and the plains of Moab, across from Jericho.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 33:42-49

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Punon (ID: `place:Punon`); Iye-abarim (ID: `place:Iye-abarim`); Almon-diblathaim (ID: `place:Almon-diblathaim`); Mount Nebo (ID: `place:NeboMount`); Dibon (ID: `place:Dibon`); Plains of Moab (ID: `place:MoabPlains`); Oboth (ID: `place:Oboth`); Jordan River (ID: `place:Jordan`); Zalmonah (ID: `place:Zalmonah`); Beth-jeshimoth (ID: `place:Beth-jeshimoth`); Moab (ID: `place:Moab`); Shittim (ID: `place:Shittim`); Jericho (ID: `place:Jericho`); Nebo (ID: `place:Nebo`)

## Numbers 33:47 (id: 22934)

Almon\-diblathaim is probably the same place as Beth\-diblathaim ([Jeremiah 48:22](https://ref.ly/Jer48:22)). This place is also mentioned in the Mesha Inscription, an ancient Moabite stone monument (see study note on Numbers 21:26).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 33:47
* **Associated Articles:** Numbers 21:26 (ID: `22776@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Almon-diblathaim (ID: `place:Almon-diblathaim`); Mount Nebo (ID: `place:NeboMount`); Nebo (ID: `place:Nebo`)

## Numbers 33:50–56 (id: 22937)

After the Israelites arrived at the plains of Moab, across from Jericho ([22:1](https://ref.ly/Num22:1)), they camped there. They remained in that place for the rest of the events recorded in Numbers (see [36:13](https://ref.ly/Num36:13); [Deuteronomy 34:8](https://ref.ly/Deut34:8)). 

While they camped there, God gave Israel specific instructions on how to deal with the people of Canaan. The Lord commanded them to destroy the people groups living in Canaan and remove all traces of their religious practices (compare [Exodus 23:23–33](https://ref.ly/Exod23:23-Exod23:33); [34:11–17](https://ref.ly/Exod34:11-Exod34:17); [Deuteronomy 7:1–6](https://ref.ly/Deut7:1-Deut7:6); [12:2–4](https://ref.ly/Deut12:2-Deut12:4); [Joshua 23:4–8](https://ref.ly/Josh23:4-Josh23:8)). 

Moses said they must destroy carved and metal idols (compare [Leviticus 26:1](https://ref.ly/Lev26:1)). They were also to tear down pagan places of worship (see [Deuteronomy 12:2–3](https://ref.ly/Deut12:2-Deut12:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 33:50-56

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Dispossess (ID: `keyterm:Dispossess`); Jordan River (ID: `place:Jordan`); Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`); To Cause to Possess (ID: `keyterm:Possess`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Plains of Moab (ID: `place:MoabPlains`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Jericho (ID: `place:Jericho`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Lot (ID: `keyterm:Lot`); Molten Image (ID: `realia:MoltenImage`); Stone Pine (ID: `flora:Pine`); Brier (ID: `flora:Brier`); High Place (ID: `realia:HighPlace`); Image (ID: `realia:Image`)

## Numbers 33:55–56 (id: 22939)

Israel did not follow God's instructions. So, they suffered the consequences predicted here ([33:51–53](https://ref.ly/Num33:51-Num33:53); see [Judges 1:1–2:5](https://ref.ly/Judg1:1-Judg2:5); [2:11–3:6](https://ref.ly/Judg2:11-Judg3:6)). Israel’s enemies in the period of the judges were like "barbs … and thorns" ([Numbers 33:55](https://ref.ly/Num33:55)). Their enemies caused them military and spiritual trouble (compare [2 Corinthians 12:7](https://ref.ly/2Cor12:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 33:55-56

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Dispossess (ID: `keyterm:Dispossess`); Brier (ID: `flora:Brier`)

## Numbers 34:1–29 (id: 22942)

[Chapter 34](https://ref.ly/Num34:1-Num34:29) describes the borders of Canaan. This is where God commanded Israel to settle, following the guidelines in [26:52–56](https://ref.ly/Num26:52-Num26:56). Since Abraham's call, the promised land of Canaan has been central to the Old Testament story ([Genesis 12:1–7](https://ref.ly/Gen12:1-Gen12:7)). A generation of Hebrews died in the wilderness for not believing what the faithful spies reported ([Numbers 13](https://ref.ly/Num13:1-Num13:33)). Yet, God reaffirmed his promise of land ([33:53](https://ref.ly/Num33:53)). 

Here, God reviews the plan and outlines the promised land's intended boundaries (compare [Joshua 13–19](https://ref.ly/Josh13:1-Josh19:51); [Ezekiel 47–48](https://ref.ly/Ezek47:1-Ezek48:35)). Old Testament Israel did not fully capture this territory. It almost did during the time of the kings David and Solomon (see [2 Samuel 24:1–9](https://ref.ly/2Sam24:1-2Sam24:9)). But these borders generally describe the region of Canaan as defined by Egyptian texts from 1500–1200 BC. This was when the Hebrews invaded. The region actually occupied by Israel changed from time to time.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 34:1-29

## Numbers 34:3–5 (id: 22948)

Portions of the southern boundary of Canaan were familiar to Israel from earlier experiences in the Wilderness of Zin, located on the northwestern border of Edom (see chapters [13–14](https://ref.ly/Num13:1-Num14:45)). This southern border curved from the southern end of the Dead Sea to the Mediterranean Sea, running east to west.

From the southern end of the Dead Sea, the boundary ran through the Ascent of Akrabbim toward Zin (compare [13:21](https://ref.ly/Num13:21)). The southernmost point was Kadesh\-barnea (see [13:26](https://ref.ly/Num13:26)). Hazar\-addar and Azmon are unknown.

## Numbers 34:5 (id: 22950)

The Brook of Egypt is not the Nile River. It refers to a wadi (a dry riverbed that fills with water during the rainy season). This wadi marked the border between Canaan and Egypt and flowed toward the Mediterranean Sea (see [1 Kings 8:65](https://ref.ly/1Kgs8:65)). I

In Old Testament times, Israel had little control over this southern coastal area.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 34:5

## Numbers 34:6–9 (id: 22951)

* The natural western boundary of Canaan was the Mediterranean coast ("the Great Sea," [34:6](https://ref.ly/Num34:6)). The northern boundary started at the Mediterranean and went east to Mount Hor. This was somewhere in the Lebanon mountain range (not the place of Aaron's death, [20:22–29](https://ref.ly/Num20:22-Num20:29)).
* *Lebo\-hamath:* Compare [13:21](https://ref.ly/Num13:21); [1 Kings 8:65](https://ref.ly/1Kgs8:65); [Amos 6:14](https://ref.ly/Amos6:14).

## Numbers 34:12 (id: 22944)

The natural eastern border of Canaan followed the Jordan River down to the Salt Sea (or Dead Sea). The Jordan River and the Salt Sea were part of the Great Rift Valley. They were strong barriers, but people could cross them. 

This boundary did not include the lands east of the Jordan River. Reuben, Gad, and half of the tribe of Manasseh lived there. These settlements were not part of Canaan. There were outside the first borders of the promised land.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 34:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Dead Sea (ID: `place:SaltSea`); Jordan River (ID: `place:Jordan`)

## Numbers 34:13–15 (id: 22945)

*Apportion this land by lot**:* Compare with [26:55–56](https://ref.ly/Num26:55-Num26:56). Two and a half tribes (Reuben, Gad, and half the tribe of Manasseh) settled in Transjordan (the region east of the Jordan River; compare with [32:33](https://ref.ly/Num32:33)). So, Canaan was to be divided among the nine and a half remaining tribes.

## Numbers 35:1–34 (id: 22952)

The tribe of Levi did not receive its own land. So chapter [35](https://ref.ly/Num35:1-Num35:34) explains how they received 48 towns spread out among the lands of the other tribes (see [Joshua 21](https://ref.ly/Josh21:1-Josh21:45); see also [Leviticus 25:32–34](https://ref.ly/Lev25:32-Lev25:34); [1 Chronicles 13:2](https://ref.ly/1Chr13:2); [2 Chronicles 11:14](https://ref.ly/2Chr11:14)). 

During the wilderness journey, the Levites lived in the center of the Israelite camp (compare [Numbers 2:17](https://ref.ly/Num2:17)). In the promised land, they were to live among the other tribes. Their presence was meant to influence the people for good (see [Deuteronomy 33:9–10](https://ref.ly/Deut33:9-Deut33:10); [2 Chronicles 17:7–9](https://ref.ly/2Chr17:7-2Chr17:9); [19:8–11](https://ref.ly/2Chr19:8-2Chr19:11); [35:3](https://ref.ly/2Chr35:3)). 

These towns and their pasturelands were not a separate homeland (compare [Numbers 18:23](https://ref.ly/Num18:23); [26:62](https://ref.ly/Num26:62)). They were only a small portion of the land of Canaan, divided among the other tribes.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 35:1-8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Refuge (ID: `keyterm:Refuge`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Levi (ID: `person:Levi.3`); Tribe of Levi (ID: `group:Levi`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jordan River (ID: `place:Jordan`); Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`); To Cause to Possess (ID: `keyterm:Possess`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Cubit (ID: `realia:Cubit`); Smear (ID: `keyterm:Smear`); Plains of Moab (ID: `place:MoabPlains`); Defilement (ID: `keyterm:Defilement`); Jericho (ID: `place:Jericho`); Appease (ID: `keyterm:Appease`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Anointing Oil (ID: `realia:AnointingOil`); Olive Oil (ID: `realia:OliveOil`)

## Numbers 35:4–5 (id: 22964)

Some have tried to explain the supposed contradiction between the 1,000 cubits *(*457 meters or 1,500 feet) and the 2,000 cubits (914 meters or 3,000 feet). One explanation is that the Hebrews thought of a town as a fixed center point. If so, the land around each town would form a square that measured 914 meters (3,000 feet) on each side. 

Another explanation is that there is a difference between the smaller pastureland mentioned in [Numbers 35:4](https://ref.ly/Num35:4) and the larger pastureland mentioned in [35:5](https://ref.ly/Num35:5). 

Based on this text, some Jewish teachers later limited how far a person could walk on the Sabbath. They said a person could walk no more than 2,000 cubits (914 meters or 3,000 feet) from home.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 35:4-5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Cubit (ID: `realia:Cubit`); Levi (ID: `person:Levi.3`); Tribe of Levi (ID: `group:Levi`)

## Numbers 35:6–34 (id: 22965)

Six of the Levites’ towns had a special purpose. They served as cities of refuge. A city of refuge was a place where a person could run for safety after accidentally killing someone.

These cities show the humanitarian (care for human life) side of Hebrew law (see also [Deuteronomy 4:41–43](https://ref.ly/Deut4:41-Deut4:43); [19:1–13](https://ref.ly/Deut19:1-Deut19:13); [Joshua 20](https://ref.ly/Josh20:1-Josh20:9)). 

These six towns offered protection when someone killed accidentally or unintentionally (compare [Exodus 21:12–14](https://ref.ly/Exod21:12-Exod21:14)). 

The 48 towns given to the Levites were spread throughout Israel. In the same way, the six cities of refuge were placed in different parts of the land. This made them easy to reach for anyone who needed safety (see [Joshua 20:7–8](https://ref.ly/Josh20:7-Josh20:8)). 

Before these cities were established, a person could sometimes seek safety by holding on to the altar. The altar was a place connected with worship (for example, [1 Kings 1:50–53](https://ref.ly/1Kgs1:50-1Kgs1:53); [2:28–34](https://ref.ly/1Kgs2:28-1Kgs2:34); compare [Exodus 21:14](https://ref.ly/Exod21:14)). 

Other ancient peoples also had places of refuge. Often these places were connected to an altar (see [Exodus 21:12–14](https://ref.ly/Exod21:12-Exod21:14); [1 Kings 2:28–34](https://ref.ly/1Kgs2:28-1Kgs2:34)). However, only ancient Israel created entire towns as places of safety. This practice shows Israel’s special concern for justice and the protection of human life.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 35:6-34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Refuge (ID: `keyterm:Refuge`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Levi (ID: `person:Levi.3`); Tribe of Levi (ID: `group:Levi`); Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jordan River (ID: `place:Jordan`); Smear (ID: `keyterm:Smear`); To Cause to Possess (ID: `keyterm:Possess`); Defilement (ID: `keyterm:Defilement`); Appease (ID: `keyterm:Appease`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Anointing Oil (ID: `realia:AnointingOil`); Olive Oil (ID: `realia:OliveOil`)

## Numbers 35:12 (id: 22953)

*a refuge from the avenger:* Justice was carried out through vengeance. A designated avenger, usually a close relative of the person who died, took this role.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 35:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Refuge (ID: `keyterm:Refuge`)

## Numbers 35:15 (id: 22955)

*the Israelites and for the foreigner or stranger among them … anyone:* The same law applied equally to everyone. Israel had one standard of justice for everyone. This was unlike many societies that had different laws for citizens and noncitizens (see also [Leviticus 19:15](https://ref.ly/Lev19:15); [Deuteronomy 1:16–17](https://ref.ly/Deut1:16-Deut1:17); [1 Timothy 5:21](https://ref.ly/1Tim5:21); [James 2:2–4](https://ref.ly/Jas2:2-Jas2:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 35:15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Refuge (ID: `keyterm:Refuge`)

## Numbers 35:15–24 (id: 22954)

* The Old Testament clearly distinguishes between intentional murder and accidental killing ([35:11](https://ref.ly/Num35:11), [22–23](https://ref.ly/Num35:22-Num35:23); [Exodus 21:12–14](https://ref.ly/Exod21:12-Exod21:14); compare [Numbers 15:22–31](https://ref.ly/Num15:22-Num15:31)). Murder required a penalty of execution, but an accidental death did not. Murder required a penalty of execution, but an accidental death did not.
* If a person used a weapon, this showed malicious intent (a plan to harm someone).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 35:15-24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Refuge (ID: `keyterm:Refuge`)

## Numbers 35:19 (id: 22956)

The avenger was the victim’s closest relative. He was responsible for putting the murderer to death*,* carrying out the vengeance that justice required ([Genesis 9:6](https://ref.ly/Gen9:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 35:19


## Numbers 35:22–23 (id: 22957)

Sometimes a person died, but the death was not caused by an intentional act of violence. In these cases, the person who caused the death did not plan or mean to kill anyone.

The cities of refuge were created as places of safety for people who were responsible for these accidental deaths. These cities protected them until the community could examine the case and decide what was just.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 35:22-23


## Numbers 35:24–25 (id: 22958)

* If the person who caused the death was found not guilty of murder at a public trial before the community (compare [Deuteronomy 19:11–12](https://ref.ly/Deut19:11-Deut19:12)), that person had to remain in the city of refuge.

    The avenger was not allowed to take revenge. The community had decided that the person was not guilty of murder.

* *until the death of the high priest**:* The death of the high priest likely served as a kind of expiation (a way of removing guilt or cleansing from sin) for the blood that had been shed. Even if the person did not commit murder, the spilling of human blood was believed to pollute the land (see [Numbers 35:33](https://ref.ly/Num35:33); compare [Genesis 4:10–11](https://ref.ly/Gen4:10-Gen4:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 35:24-25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Smear (ID: `keyterm:Smear`); Refuge (ID: `keyterm:Refuge`); Anointing Oil (ID: `realia:AnointingOil`); Olive Oil (ID: `realia:OliveOil`)

## Numbers 35:26–29 (id: 22959)

The killer (*manslayer)* had to stay in a special city (called a "city of refuge") as a consequence for the death he had caused. He had to stay in a city of refuge until he or the high priest died. After the high priest's death, the *manslayer* could return home, and the *avenger of blood* could not take revenge.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 35:26-29

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Refuge (ID: `keyterm:Refuge`)

## Numbers 35:30 (id: 22960)

All murderers were condemned to death*,* but their conviction required a high standard of evidence. This included testimony from two or more witnesses (compare [Deuteronomy 17:6](https://ref.ly/Deut17:6); [19:15](https://ref.ly/Deut19:15)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 35:30


## Numbers 35:31–32 (id: 22961)

The law did not allow the payment of a ransom (money paid as compensation) to free a convicted murderer from execution. The death penalty was required for murder (compare [2 Samuel 21:2–9](https://ref.ly/2Sam21:2-2Sam21:9)). Similarly, a person who caused an accidental death could not pay money to avoid staying in a city of refuge. That person had to remain there until the proper time.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 35:31-32

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Refuge (ID: `keyterm:Refuge`)

## Numbers 35:33 (id: 22962)

*for bloodshed pollutes the land:* If the person who caused a death, whether murder or accidental killing, was not treated as the law commanded, God viewed the land as polluted.

Human bloodshed required death as a response ([Genesis 4:10–11](https://ref.ly/Gen4:10-Gen4:11); [9:6](https://ref.ly/Gen9:6)). This rule is different from cases such as [Exodus 21:29–30](https://ref.ly/Exod21:29-Exod21:30) (see also [Leviticus 18:28](https://ref.ly/Lev18:28); [Ezekiel 36:17–18](https://ref.ly/Ezek36:17-Ezek36:18); [Hosea 4:2–3](https://ref.ly/Hos4:2-Hos4:3)). 

This strong warning about murder shows the great value of human life ([Exodus 20:13](https://ref.ly/Exod20:13)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 35:33

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Appease (ID: `keyterm:Appease`)

## Numbers 35:34 (id: 22963)

The Lord lived among the people of Israel while the Tent of Meeting (the tabernacle) was in the center of the Hebrew camp (see chapters [1–4](https://ref.ly/Num1:1-Num4:49)). This meant that they must not defile their land. They could not ignore God’s commands. They also could not fail to carry out justice as the law required (compare [5:1–4](https://ref.ly/Num5:1-Num5:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 35:34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Defilement (ID: `keyterm:Defilement`)

## Numbers 36:1–4 (id: 22967)

Women who inherited land might marry men from another tribe*.* Their land would then be lost by their ancestral tribe and transferred to their husband’s tribe. 

The "Year of Jubilee" usually allowed land to return to its original owner ([Leviticus 25:8–55](https://ref.ly/Lev25:8-Lev25:55)). But it only applied to land sold, not land gained through marriage.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 36:1-4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); To Cause to Possess (ID: `keyterm:Possess`); Lot (ID: `keyterm:Lot`); Zelophehad (ID: `person:Zelophehad`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Manasseh (ID: `person:Manasseh`); Gilead (ID: `place:Gilead`); Tribe of Manasseh (ID: `group:Manasseh`); Machir (ID: `person:Machir`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Year of Acquittance (ID: `keyterm:YearOfAcquittance`)

## Numbers 36:1–13 (id: 22966)

The five daughters of Zelophehad requested and received fair property rights ([27:1–11](https://ref.ly/Num27:1-Num27:11)). This passage discusses the effects of that decision.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 36:1-13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); To Cause to Possess (ID: `keyterm:Possess`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Zelophehad (ID: `person:Zelophehad`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Lot (ID: `keyterm:Lot`); Manasseh (ID: `person:Manasseh`); Tribe of Manasseh (ID: `group:Manasseh`); Noah (ID: `person:Noah.2`); Gilead (ID: `place:Gilead`); Jordan River (ID: `place:Jordan`); Milcah (ID: `person:Milcah.2`); Dispossess (ID: `keyterm:Dispossess`); Mahlah (ID: `person:Mahlah`); Plains of Moab (ID: `place:MoabPlains`); Tirzah (ID: `person:Tirzah`); Jericho (ID: `place:Jericho`); Year of Acquittance (ID: `keyterm:YearOfAcquittance`); Hoglah (ID: `person:Hoglah`); Machir (ID: `person:Machir`)

## Numbers 36:5 (id: 22970)

The Lord quickly provided the justice that the men of the tribe of Joseph asked for in their request.

Here, *the tribe of Joseph* refers to the tribe of Manasseh, the son of Joseph.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 36:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Numbers 36:6–9 (id: 22971)

These rules solved the problem. They ensured that each tribe would keep its land.

In this way, the land given to each tribe would remain stable and continue within that tribe for future generations.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 36:6-9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Cause to Possess (ID: `keyterm:Possess`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Zelophehad (ID: `person:Zelophehad`); Dispossess (ID: `keyterm:Dispossess`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Numbers 36:10–12 (id: 22968)

Zelophehad’s five daughters married their cousins on their father's side, as instructed. This kept their father's land within his clan. 

In ancient Hebrew society, marrying a first cousin was acceptable (compare [Leviticus 18](https://ref.ly/Lev18:1-Lev18:30), [20](https://ref.ly/Lev20:1-Lev20:27); see also [1 Chronicles 23:22](https://ref.ly/1Chr23:22)). Other Near Eastern cultures also accepted this practice.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 36:10-12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Zelophehad (ID: `person:Zelophehad`); Noah (ID: `person:Noah.2`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Hoglah (ID: `person:Hoglah`); Mahlah (ID: `person:Mahlah`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Manasseh (ID: `person:Manasseh`); To Cause to Possess (ID: `keyterm:Possess`); Tirzah (ID: `person:Tirzah`); Tribe of Manasseh (ID: `group:Manasseh`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Milcah (ID: `person:Milcah.2`)

## Numbers 36:13 (id: 22969)

* *commands and ordinances:* These might refer to the legal instructions in the whole book (compare [Leviticus 27:34](https://ref.ly/Lev27:34)). They might also refer only to the laws given on the plains of Moab ([Numbers 26–36](https://ref.ly/Num26:1-Num36:13)).
* After this, only one step remained before the Israelites entered Canaan. Moses needed to remind them of their history and their covenant responsibilities. These are the main themes of the book of Deuteronomy.

* **Associated Passages:** Numbers 36:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Plains of Moab (ID: `place:MoabPlains`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Jericho (ID: `place:Jericho`); Jordan River (ID: `place:Jordan`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`)
